## Lookup 1: haiku game cf185ab2, player_8 the necromancer, day_vote, day 3
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 fortune_teller, player_2 trailseer, player_3 healer, player_4 sentinel, player_5 chanteuse, player_6 vigilante, player_7 investigator, player_8 necromancer, player_9 sigilist, player_10 illusionist

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 3 after two abstain days: a claimed sentinel named a single visitor to a dead trailseer on night 1, and the accused player is demanding the sentinel explain it, while the village debates whether to vote on either. I have two dead bodies (a trailseer and a vigilante) whose night abilities I could later use, which the village does not know about. Information landscape: Information-starved: one unverified sentinel claim with one visit, two day-2 votes with no stated evidence, and no confirmed results; the only hard datum is that a single visit reached a dead trailseer on night 1. Stakes: Eight alive with four eliminations until the leading evil faction can win; a single wrong vote is recoverable but costly, and the game cannot end within one more elimination, so keeping visibility low this day preserves room for later body use. Consensus: The room is split with no clear consensus; two abstain days have passed with no lynch, and the discussion centers on the claimed sentinel's single visit claim rather than any agreed target. My position: With the majority on the abstain side in practice, holding out of the early accusations and not driving any lynch. Heat: Moderate and rising: I voted to eliminate a player on day 2 who then abstained, and one accuser has said my day-2 vote named no one substantive, so I am a visible but not focal target. Forward exposure: Any visible move (a vote, a claim, a body-use hint) commits me to a stance that cannot be undone once read by the village; the day-3 vote is public and permanent, so voting with the majority keeps my visibility low. Target landscape: Two accusers and one claimed sentinel are in play: a claimed sentinel (unverified) whose single watch result names one visitor, a player being voted on for a day-2 push with no new evidence, and a player accused by two others with only a stated night-1 reason; all bases are claims and day-2 votes, not confirmed roles. Public vs private: As the necromancer I privately know my own role and that I am not visible to anyone's night checks, while the public sees only a day-3 accusation thread between a claimed sentinel and the player who accused them; my private knowledge is that I am the lone killer and no one on the table can verify that.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 532ee37c-8e55-476e-ab15-b4f7d2856b71 (score 0.822) *When:* Day-3 vote after a night kill, often with a concealed or contested body. The village has converged on the necromancer for behavior (silence or a walked-back accusation) while other players dispute visits and roleblocks. Information landscape: Mixed to thin. There are unverified claims and a visit dispute, but no confirmed wolf or killer. The necromancer's best information comes from a body-watch or similar result that it cannot disclose without exposing its role. Stakes: Mid-game with eight alive: an elimination of the lone killer ends its win path. Each vote matters, since a lone vote leaves the majority on you unopposed and you are removed. Consensus: The room is concentrating on the necromancer for behavior, not for evidence. The remaining votes are scattered across contested claims. My position: Holding out alone against the room and casting a vote that nobody else shares. Heat: High. You were the main target on the previous day, and several voters again aim at you for silence or an unsupported accusation you had to concede. Forward exposure: A lone vote on a read nobody shares commits you publicly to an unsupported position. It adds nothing to any vote count, does not lower your heat, and is permanent and irreversible. A vote that joins an existing bloc costs little and can be explained. Target landscape: Several candidates (sentinel, healer, trailseer, illusionist, sigilist) with contested claims and no hard evidence. The case against you is behavioral, resting on silence or an overstated accusation. Cases against the others rest on disputes or private results you cannot share. Public vs private: Privately you are the lone killer, or hold unshareable body-derived information. Publicly you are a repeat target whose reads are unsourced, so a solo vote looks isolated and odd.
  *Do:* On a day-3 vote where the room is concentrating on you, don't spend your vote on a lone-voter target nobody shares. Pick the candidate who already has the most votes among the people pressing you, or one the room already doubts, so your vote joins a visible bloc. Pair it with the one claim you can defend publicly, because a vote you cannot source or explain stays on the permanent record and cannot be undone.
- key b3853b08-04ac-48dd-9c2a-a41a05b48bf1 (score 0.818) *When:* Day 2 is the first vote of the game. The room has piled onto one accused player, either a claimant whose result contradicts the rules or a player placed at a victim's door by claimed results. You are the necromancer, newly past your immunity night and with no hidden visits exposed. Information landscape: Information-thin: unverified role claims, perhaps one claimed result or rules contradiction to anchor on, and few or no confirmed roles beyond early deaths. Stakes: Early game with many players alive; a single vote does not flip the outcome. The priority is staying hidden while the village removes a player, and a revealed body adds to your night options. Consensus: The village is nearly unanimous, 8-1 or 9 votes, behind one accused player, based on a claimed report, logical contradiction, or weak defense rather than a wolf-driven push. My position: With the majority, voting alongside the room. Heat: Low to almost none: you have been quiet or have only asked mild questions or endorsed a report, and nobody has accused you or tied a night event to you. Forward exposure: A vote with the majority reveals nothing about your role and commits you only to the room's consensus. It is public and permanent, but unremarkable while the room is unanimous; a lone dissent would draw attention. Target landscape: One candidate has clear traction, accused on a claimed watch or visit report, a rules contradiction, or a weak defense. Other players hold unverified but unchallenged claims. No roles are confirmed, so the basis is claims and logic rather than hard evidence. Public vs private: Privately you know you are the lone killer with no wolf allegiance and no exposed visits; publicly you look like one more town-leaning voter and nobody is looking at you.
  *Do:* On the first day vote, when the room has converged on one accused player and you are not under suspicion, vote with the majority for that player instead of splitting off or floating a new target. A bandwagon vote reveals nothing about your role and keeps heat near zero. Any dead player whose role is announced then becomes a usable body for your later night abilities.
- key 6d6cd815-ae6a-4a11-83dc-3ef24ac11605 (score 0.812) *When:* Endgame vote at about five players alive. The wolves are gone, a necromancer's night attacks through dead bodies have been publicly identified, and the town is voting to find the lone killer by elimination. Information landscape: Information-rich: dead roles were revealed, a will or a sigil strike cleared some players, claims are known and votes are recorded, so the necromancer's choices are scrutinized. Stakes: At five alive, one wrong elimination or one night kill leaves the necromancer close to the at-most-one-other-alive win. One exposed vote can also cause the necromancer's own elimination or a night shot, so each ballot has decisive weight. Consensus: The town has narrowed to a small suspect pool by elimination and claims, and the leading confirmed players have converged on one target. My position: holding out against the forming consensus Heat: Moderate to extreme: the necromancer is among a handful of suspects, with some cover from a dead investigator's clearance but questions about its votes and about pressure on specific claims. Forward exposure: A vote against the player the leading confirmed players are defending, or a lone vote against a quiet player, is public and permanent and cannot be undone. It commits you to a stance that the town will later read as deflection or antagonism. Target landscape: Few survivors, among them a confirmed vigilante, a speculator, a claimed healer and a claimed sigilist. The leading players favor one target on the basis of claims and behavior. The alternative target is backed only by the player with the most to hide. Public vs private: Privately the necromancer knows they are the lone killer and that the reanimated attacks came from them; the town knows only that a necromancer exists among the survivors and is reading each vote and defense as a tell.
  *Do:* When five players remain, both wolves are dead and the town is hunting the lone killer, vote with the room's strongest evidence-backed consensus (the confirmed, leading players' target) instead of holding out for a different target. If the consensus target is someone else and no evidence clears you, joining it keeps your ballot from standing out as a deflection, which matters because the town is reading votes for deflection.
- key f8419b5e-b6fe-4f18-b8f4-7cb58aeca378 (score 0.786) *When:* Day 2 vote after a single night death, with no investigative results shared. Votes are scattered, and the quiet players, you included, draw suspicion for silence alone. Information landscape: Information-starved: one night death, a few claims, and no verified ties to wolves or a killer. Reads are behavioral only. Stakes: Early game with many players alive, so no single vote decides the game. The main risk is attracting lasting suspicion before the necromancer's first usable night. Consensus: The village leans toward abstaining because no checks, watches or follows have been shared. Support for any elimination is thin and scattered. My position: With the majority when abstaining; holding out if you cast a lone vote. Heat: Low to moderate. Any pressure rests on silence, with no concrete evidence against you. Forward exposure: A named vote goes on the permanent public record and can be questioned for days; an abstain reveals nothing and commits you to nothing. Target landscape: All living players are unconfirmed apart from the dead player's reveal. The only candidates are quiet players, chosen on silence rather than evidence. Public vs private: Privately you are the lone killer with no night information yet (Night 1 is inactive, so you read Not suspicious). Publicly you are an unconfirmed, quiet player, and the room has no evidence against anyone.
  *Do:* On an early day-vote with no hard evidence and a room leaning toward abstaining, abstain with the majority instead of casting a lone named vote. Abstaining commits you to nothing and keeps your vote on the public record from looking aggressive or unexplained. If a plurality is forming on you, still abstain and let the abstain bloc block it, since a counter-vote would not change the tally.

**Situation the seat wrote:** The opening night removed one player through both a wolf attack and a serial-killer attack, while your concealment withheld that player's role and will. This leaves the village without the dead player's role information as day two begins, while you privately know the concealed player was the Vigilante. Information landscape: Information-starved publicly: the attacks and concealment are confirmed, but the victim's role and will are unavailable to the village. You alone know the victim was the Vigilante; there are no discussion claims or accusations yet today. Stakes: Nine players are alive; five eliminations from the leading evil faction's win threshold, at worst for the town because the concealed body's side is unknown. The game cannot end within one more elimination, at worst for the town, so a single result is not yet a swing. Consensus: No current village alignment is visible: today's discussion has not started, and the previous summary records no accusations. My position: No position relative to a forming village consensus is established; there is no current discussion or consensus to align with or oppose. Heat: No suspicion of you is recorded in today's discussion, which has not begun; the public record contains no accusation against you. Forward exposure: A visible claim about the concealed player's role or the hidden kill trace would commit you to information the village cannot verify during play and could expose your private knowledge. No such public claim or other visible move is currently on record. Target landscape: No candidates are currently being debated; there are no public role reveals or claims to compare. The only confirmed event is that the concealed player was attacked by both the wolves and the serial killer and died. Public vs private: The public knows that the player killed by both the wolves and the serial killer had their role concealed. Privately, you know they were the Vigilante and that you concealed the body; the village has no public role or will from them.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key c1f4f53c-dd94-42a4-a27a-3ccc078296fe (score 0.8) *When:* Day 2 opens after a night-1 death or attack announcement. Town is sorting unverified night-result claims and has no suspect, while you are a wolf illusionist with conceals in hand and no exposure. Information landscape: Information-starved: one death or attack announcement, perhaps a revealed role, and a few unverified claims. There are no vote records and no caught lies. Stakes: Early game with many players alive, so parity is far off and no single day-2 vote decides the game. Staying unnoticed keeps the pack's conceals and kills available for later, higher-stakes days. Consensus: The village is loosely aligned on a shared theme such as lost information, protecting info-holders or asking claimants for explanations. It has no firm target and tends toward an abstain. My position: With the majority or on the sidelines, echoing the room's line without driving a target. Heat: Minimal to none: you have not been accused or linked to night events, and the pressure is on other claimants or on nobody. Forward exposure: A vague or procedural comment commits you to no read and is fully reversible. Any concrete accusation, defense or claim about night events could imply inside knowledge and tie you or your partner to the kill. Target landscape: No confirmed suspect. The candidates are an unverified claimant, a player tracked near the pack's target, and a quiet field. The basis is behavior and unverified claims, not hard evidence. Public vs private: You privately know what the pack did on night 1: which kill landed, which failed, and whether your partner's block matched a public claim. Publicly the village sees only a death or an attack announcement and unverified claims, so your private knowledge sits behind a thin public read.
  *Do:* On an information-starved Day 2 with no heat on you, make one brief comment that echoes the room's existing theme or a neutral procedural question, such as asking a claimant to commit to a target or asking a tracked player to explain. Do not push a target, do not touch the pack's kill or the failed night kill, and stay with the majority; if the room starts to turn toward you or your partner, switch to a short sympathetic line that reopens nothing.
- key ecf8b24f-e086-4b2e-8d1a-e1a44eadb827 (score 0.774) *When:* Day 2 or 3 after a night kill. Town claims are on the table and in dispute, a wolf partner may be mildly exposed, and the illusionist is unsuspected with conceals still unspent. Information landscape: Thin, unverified claims and visit reports, with no game-master confirmation beyond dead players' roles. Information-starved and mostly self-reported. Stakes: The pack is not near parity, so there is no pressure to force anything. Staying alive and unlinked to the kill matters more than winning any single argument, and your remaining conceals are worth preserving. Consensus: The room is focused on claim conflicts such as visit matches or counter-claims, and is loosely aligned on a target other than the illusionist. My position: With the majority or on the sidelines, not driving any accusation. Heat: Essentially none: you have not been named, accused, or tied to the night kill. Forward exposure: A clarifying question commits you to nothing and is reversible. An accusation or a defense of your partner would put your read on the permanent record and could link you to the kill or to her. Target landscape: Several town claimants (investigator, sentinel, trailseer, healer) and a few quiet players, with no confirmed roles. Everything is behavioral or self-reported, and nothing separates wolves from town. Public vs private: You privately know your partner's role, that the pack made the night kill, and that you hold your conceals. Publicly the town sees only unverified claims and a dead player, with no link to you.
  *Do:* When you are an unsuspected illusionist wolf in an info-starved day with several unverified town claims, keep your contribution to at most one neutral clarifying question about a claim, or stay silent. Add no accusations or defenses of your partner, so your name stays off the record while the room argues among itself. If your partner comes under fire, let the vote follow the majority rather than defending her openly.
- key fedb45cd-12ff-46cf-ac69-a1d196c80d95 (score 0.744) *When:* Your wolf partner died and their role was revealed. You concealed a body the previous night, and the town opens discussion by examining who voted alongside the dead wolf, which points straight at you. Information landscape: Information-rich: a confirmed dead wolf, a concealed body, and permanent public voting records showing coordination between you and the dead wolf. Stakes: You are the last wolf, so an elimination vote on you ends the wolf team. Surviving this day decides the game. Consensus: The town is converging on you. Its basis is the voting record, and it treats distancing language from you as a typical wolf move. My position: Holding out, alone, against a forming consensus on you. Heat: Extreme. The only hard evidence in the room, a shared public vote with a confirmed wolf, points at you. Forward exposure: Ignoring the shared vote and praising the Investigator committed you to a distancing stance and left the vote unexplained. A direct explanation is also visible and hard to retract, but it answers the main evidence against you. Target landscape: You are the primary suspect because of the vote you shared with the dead wolf. Other living players, such as the healer or a neutral, carry only minor behavioral suspicion. Public vs private: You know you are the last wolf and what the concealed body was. The town only sees a confirmed wolf who voted alongside you, and it is building a link from the public vote record.
  *Do:* When your partner is revealed dead and the room starts tracing who voted with them, address the shared vote first and plainly. Give the specific public reason you voted that way, and say how you would have voted had you known their role. Do not use flattery of a town player (such as praising the Investigator) to change the subject, because the public vote record stays in play until you account for it.
- key e2657fe7-6c5f-4d3b-9e3d-9ee21e4150d0 (score 0.735) *When:* Early day discussion after a night kill where town information roles are comparing night actions and the illusionist, a hidden wolf, must take part without drawing scrutiny. Information landscape: Thin and mostly self-reported. Results are unpublished or unverified, so the room is working from claims and behavioral reads, and the illusionist holds private pack knowledge. Stakes: The board is mid-game with the wolves not yet near parity, so one exposed wolf can still be afforded but costs a conceal-capable member of the pack. Staying unnoticed preserves both remaining conceals. Consensus: The town is loosely aligned on examining claimants' stories and night-one actions, with no verified leads and no decided target. My position: with majority Heat: Low to none at the start of the day, rising only if the illusionist is silent while trackers or others ask for participation. Forward exposure: A short, non-claiming contribution commits the illusionist to nothing falsifiable and can be dropped or redirected later. Total silence is also free of claims, but it becomes the most visible thing about the illusionist and is hard to undo once trackers name it. Target landscape: Several town information-role claimants (investigator, sentinel, trailseer, vigilante or healer claimants) are sharing or probing night results. Attention can be steered to a claimant with an early lone vote or a disputed roleblock story, based on behavior rather than hard evidence. Public vs private: Privately the illusionist knows the pack's kill and the wolves' night actions, while the public has only unverified claims and behavioral reads, with the illusionist's role unsuspected.
  *Do:* When the room is trading night results, speak early with a brief, low-commitment contribution, such as sympathy for the dead and a prompt for others to share visit directions. Claim no falsifiable result and make no vote commitment, so you stay active in the discussion without a mechanical claim that could be checked. If a visit-tracking role is present, a silent illusionist is the easiest person to question, so speak in the opening round.

## Lookup 3: haiku game dcb8016a, player_6 the speculator, day_vote, day 4
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 sentinel, player_3 illusionist, player_4 sigilist, player_5 chanteuse, player_6 speculator, player_7 necromancer, player_8 vigilante, player_9 investigator, player_10 trailseer

**Situation the seat wrote:** A claimed Sentinel's night-3 watch shows only player_5 at player_1's door, while player_1 says they were roleblocked that night, so the block is unexplained; player_5 admits the visit, denies being blocked, and claims no role. Several players are pressing a vote on player_5 while the accusation rests on one watch record against one block report. Information landscape: Information-starved: one unverified sentinel watch, one roleblock report with no source, and one admitted visit; no confirmed role claims survive and no night results have been announced beyond the dead players' roles. Stakes: Six alive with four eliminations until the leading evil faction can win; a single wrong lynch is recoverable but costly, since it would bring the evil side closer to parity and the wolves and necromancer remain unidentified. Consensus: No clear village consensus; the room is split between a vote on player_5 and calls to test the sentinel claim further, with the accusers relying on the watch record and the accused relying on the block report contradiction. My position: Holding out from the forming pressure on player_5, with a low profile and no night result of my own. Heat: Moderate on me as the Speculator: I have not been heavily accused, but I am a living voter whose pick is already announced as Town, so my vote is watched for alignment with the Town pick. Target landscape: Player_5 (unrevealed, living) who admits a night-3 visit to player_1 with no role claimed; player_1 (unrevealed, living) who reports being roleblocked that night; player_2 (claimed Sentinel, unverified, living) whose night-3 watch shows only player_5 at player_1's door. Public role status: no one confirmed beyond the dead.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 2d0b01cd-5c47-4a73-826e-1b902245d25f (score 0.827) *When:* After a night kill (often hidden-role or double attack), a role claimant's night report places one named player at the victim's door. The accused denies it and attacks the claimants, and the room converges on the accused. The speculator has already picked Town or is about to. Information landscape: Moderately to highly info-rich: specific watch or follow claims that corroborate one another, visitor counts, an investigator read, and caught mechanics errors, though nothing is officially confirmed. Stakes: Mid-game with several evil threats still alive. Removing a wolf or the lone killer by an evidence-backed vote shifts the board toward Town and toward the speculator's Town pick, and a wrong eviction would cost a Town member. Consensus: Largely aligned (most of the living players) behind the evidence-backed target. Only the accused and perhaps one wolf partner dissent. My position: With the majority, lightly helping build the case with a verification question or a public argument. Heat: Low: not accused, only asking questions or backing the majority line. Target landscape: Two or three suspects: one accused by independent, partly corroborating night claims (watch report, trailseer follow, visitor list, investigator read) who denies and counter-accuses, versus the accuser(s), who are unverified but consistent with each other. Evidence outweighs behavior on the accused, and nobody is confirmed by the Game Master.
  *Do:* On a day-vote with low heat, vote with the evidence-backed majority on the player whose story is contradicted by independent night reports (a watch claim, a follow match, a visitor count, a counter-claim) rather than on mood. If you have not yet picked, ask for a Trailseer or Sentinel to corroborate before the vote, then vote with the room. Each eviction of a wolf or lone killer moves Town toward winning, which is what your Town pick needs.
- key c296637d-c496-45de-9fda-e3800c55c153 (score 0.824) *When:* Day 2 first vote: a claimed power role admitted their reported result looked wrong and the whole table is accusing them, while you have not yet made your side pick. Information landscape: Information-starved: one self-admitted inconsistency and unverified claims; no confirmed roles. Stakes: Early elimination of a Town role weakens Town's information while the serial killer and wolves are still active; a mistaken unanimous vote is hard to reverse and your own pick depends on Town surviving. Consensus: Near-unanimous pile-on against one claimed role based on a single reported inconsistency; the room is driven by claim analysis, not verified facts. My position: With the majority and one of the voices driving it. Heat: Low; you are an unclaimed voice agreeing with the majority. Target landscape: One claimed Sentinel under real pressure after reporting a watch result that omitted their own visit; the other claimant (a trailseer) looks consistent. No competing suspects and no hard evidence beyond the claim's inconsistency.
  *Do:* When the table is piling onto a single claimed role over a stated inconsistency and there is no stronger lead, vote with the consensus only after checking that the inconsistency is a real rules violation rather than a misstatement. If the rule check is unclear, abstain, so you do not help eliminate a likely Town power role while you still hold your pick.
- key 2de3fdf8-f717-43cf-9b8e-a48592ae3952 (score 0.8) *When:* Four players alive with one wolf remaining; a dispute between two info claimants over whether a watch counts as a visit, and the room votes out a claimed role over an apparent rules error. Information landscape: Rich in claim histories but poisoned by a rules dispute, so the evidence is contested rather than confirmed. Stakes: At four alive with a wolf left, one wrong elimination of a real Town role gives the wolf parity and ends the game; the vote is decisive. Consensus: Majority formed against the claimed sentinel on a disputed rules argument, with the vigilante holding the decisive bullet. My position: With the majority, joining a vote that was already forming. Heat: Moderate: the claimed vigilante questioned the speculator's lack of a role claim, though attention quickly moved to the dispute between information claimants. Target landscape: Claimed sentinel accused of lying about watching being a visit, versus a claimed trailseer and claimed vigilante pushing the accusation; the case rests on a rules dispute and counter-accusation, not hard evidence.
  *Do:* With a wolf still alive and a dispute between claimed info roles, do not just join the majority on a vote over a rules-interpretation quarrel. Check the accusation against the stated rules (for example, a watch is a visit to the target but is not shown in the watcher's own result) and vote for the player whose claim actually conflicts with the rules or other evidence, or hold out if the accusation is only a semantic quarrel.
- key 12a3659f-65ff-486a-ab83-76d6cd42cc97 (score 0.798) *When:* Day 3 vote after a night kill by wolves or the serial killer; the village converges on a single accused player on corroborating night-result claims, and the speculator has the option to join the vote that decides whether the remaining evil is removed. Information landscape: Reasonably information-rich: a wolf is already confirmed dead or revealed, there are corroborating tracking claims and a clean investigator check, and the accused offers only blanket denials. Stakes: With wolves or the lone killer nearly gone, one more correct elimination can end or nearly end the game; the vote is rarely decisive alone but it fixes the board toward a Town win, which is the side the speculator pick favors. Consensus: Largely aligned: a pack of accusers forms on matching tracking and check reports plus the reveals of dead wolves. My position: With the majority, joining the pile-on early or at the last minute rather than holding out. Heat: Low to moderate: no accusation round is aimed at you, though the accused may have counter-accused or voted against you. Target landscape: One accused player named by matching independent tracking or check claims (trailseer, sentinel, investigator); the claims are unverified but agree with each other and with confirmed reveals. The alternative candidates have no case against them; the accused's defense is blanket denial or counter-accusation.
  *Do:* On a day-3 vote where several tracking or check reports (trailseer, sentinel, investigator) converge on one accused and confirmed wolves are already known, vote with that convergent group for the accused instead of an unrelated player. Removing a convergent target sharpens the endgame toward Town, which pays off a Town pick; if you still hold your pick, the converging reports are your cue to lock in the side that now looks to be winning.
- key 22fc6adf-8e37-44f9-88af-2a6333e22e88 (score 0.783) *When:* Late-game day vote at five alive where the vote is split or tied. The speculator's ballot decides whether a useful Town-aligned role survives, and the speculator has already picked a side. Information landscape: Mixed. Some night save announcements and claimed information-role reports exist, but they conflict, and several dead players' roles are hidden. No role is verified. Stakes: At five alive, one elimination can bring evil to parity. A single ballot decides between keeping a protective role alive and handing the wolves or lone killer a free removal. Consensus: Fractured: votes split between two or more targets, with no majority and a tie likely, resting on conflicting claimed visit reports. My position: Holding the swing or deciding ballot, able to join a wagon or drive an alternative target. Heat: Moderate to high: the speculator is accused or voted for by others based on visit reports, though in some cases heat is only low to moderate. Target landscape: Three or four candidates at five alive: a claimed healer with a confirmed save announcement, a claimed trailseer or sentinel, the speculator under accusation, and a quiet unclaimed player. Evidence is claims and visit reconstructions, not confirmed roles.
  *Do:* With about five players alive and the vote split or tied, use your ballot to protect a claimed protective or information role whose behavior and announced results fit. Aim it at the least-evidenced quiet player, since a tie or no elimination keeps the Town-aligned pick alive. If the room is scattered and no role is confirmed, spend it on the most defensible suspect rather than a token vote.
- key eb1c19f5-88cb-42a1-9ef5-2196166bd9c5 (score 0.768) *When:* A rival counter-claims the Speculator role or publicly ties your pattern of actions to it, and calls your claim late or false. You have to hold your claim while the room decides who the wolf is. Information landscape: Role-uniqueness contradiction plus elimination reasoning from dead roles and Town results. Evidence is moderately rich, although the targeted player's role is not confirmed until the vote. Stakes: Late game with few players left. If the wrong claimant is eliminated, evil can reach parity. Removing the false claimant can end the game. Consensus: The room is mostly backing your claim or already turning on the accuser. The shared basis is the unique-role contradiction and the narrowed suspect pool. My position: With the majority, partly driving it as the original claimant. Heat: Moderate to elevated. A rival claimed your role, or someone linked your actions to the Speculator, and at least one vote has been cast against you. Target landscape: The counter-claimant or the player pressing on your role, versus a few players already cleared by Town results or confirmed by elimination. Evidence is a mix of role uniqueness, elimination logic, and behavior.
  *Do:* If someone counter-claims your Speculator role or ties your behavior to it, keep your claim consistent and point out that there is only one Speculator. Then vote for the counter-claimant or pressurer if the cleared roles and the elimination logic leave them as the remaining evil candidate. This works because the Town pick is already announced and a counter-claimant is forced into being the wolf or a liar.

My position: You have made no public statement and have not taken a visible position in the discussion; you are not driving or publicly opposing the restraint expressed by others. Heat: No one has accused or questioned you in the public discussion; suspicion on you is low, with no stated basis. All living role claims are unverified claims. Stakes: Six alive with two eliminations until the leading evil faction can win, and the game cannot end within one more elimination, so a single wrong lynch is recoverable but narrows the margin for the remaining wolves and the Serial Killer. Forward exposure: Any visible move by the agent (naming a player, claiming a result, or voting against a claimed info role) commits it publicly; a vote is permanent on the record and cannot be undone, while a verbal claim can be contradicted later by another claimant. **Lessons to judge:**
- key 672e1a00-978e-41eb-831f-6041f616a334 (score 0.82) *When:* Three players are alive after a daytime elimination, and one night kill by the serial killer brings the count to two. Information landscape: Information-rich: published watch results, a confirmed wolf elimination, and roles largely narrowed down. Stakes: The next kill ends the game, so the SK wins by killing any one of the other two and loses a chance if it fails to kill. Forward exposure: Terminal and irreversible: the kill ends the game immediately, so there is no later exposure to manage. Target landscape: Two other players remain: a claimed town information role (e.g. sentinel) and a quiet player who voted against the SK. Either kill wins; the claimed town role is the safest pick since it is the most likely to hold useful information or protection.
  *Do:* With three alive, you plus two others, and any single kill ending the game, attack whichever other player is alive and can be reached, preferably the one with a role that could otherwise stop you. This reaches the two-alive win condition at once, so no day vote follows.
- key ff56c3ad-aa50-464e-a626-fde3a350a64b (score 0.807) *When:* Night 3 with the wolves collapsing (one or both revealed or dead). Forward exposure: Each announced kill is permanent and adds to the attacker count. A duplicate or saved attack on the same player reveals a second attacker and removes nobody. Once the wolves are gone, the village treats the lone killer as the only unexplained attacker, and the choice cannot be undone. Target landscape: Surviving candidates are the claimed sentinel, trailseer, healer, vigilante, sigilist and a neutral. The sentinel was picked on behavior (accusing or naming you) in both cases where the wolves and a healer covered the same player. No target had hard evidence tying them to you.
  *Do:* On the night the wolves are collapsing, pick a victim the wolves are unlikely to hit, rather than the loudest accuser or the most trusted information role. A quiet player who could still vote you out or punish you later (sigilist, vigilante) fits. If the obvious target is the sentinel or another public figure, assume the wolves and a healer converge there and shift your kill elsewhere.

Claims about night visits are unverified. **Lessons to judge:**
- key 3f8b4fa3-adfe-42d2-beac-ad6b46bde56e (score 0.759) *When:* First day with a vote, after a night-1 death. There are unverified role claims and counter-claims, or no claims at all. Information landscape: Information-starved: one dead player with a known role, unverified night-result claims, no confirmed wolf, and no voting history yet. Stakes: Early game with many players alive, so one vote does not flip the winning side. A wrongful elimination of a real Town role is costly, and your abstain avoids adding to it. Your win depends only on the later pick. Consensus: The room is split or only loosely aligned. It is either a universal abstain or a near-majority piling onto one player, based on behavior and unverified claims rather than confirmed roles. My position: Holding out by abstaining, either with an abstain bloc or against a bandwagon, without driving any accusation. Heat: Low to none: you have been quiet or only echoed the room's questions, and no one has accused you. Target landscape: Two or more candidates drawing votes, such as a quiet player with no claim, a claimed healer, or rival investigator claimants. Their claims are unverified. The only evidence is a night-1 sentinel or trailseer report or a visit link, and everything else is behavior.
  *Do:* On the first vote day, while your one pick is unused and no candidate has hard evidence against them (only unverified claims, quietness, or a bandwagon), abstain rather than join the pile-on. If a candidate has a concrete contradiction, such as a caught lie or a claim that conflicts with an announced fact, vote for that player instead. If abstaining has been removed after two no-elimination days, vote for the best-supported suspect. Abstaining keeps your heat low, and your win depends on the pick, not on this vote.
- key 2d0b01cd-5c47-4a73-826e-1b902245d25f (score 0.745) *When:* After a night kill (often hidden-role or double attack), a role claimant's night report places one named player at the victim's door. Information landscape: Moderately to highly info-rich: specific watch or follow claims that corroborate one another, visitor counts, an investigator read, and caught mechanics errors, though nothing is officially confirmed. Stakes: Mid-game with several evil threats still alive. Removing a wolf or the lone killer by an evidence-backed vote shifts the board toward Town and toward the speculator's Town pick, and a wrong eviction would cost a Town member. Consensus: Largely aligned (most of the living players) behind the evidence-backed target. Only the accused and perhaps one wolf partner dissent. My position: With the majority, lightly helping build the case with a verification question or a public argument. Heat: Low: not accused, only asking questions or backing the majority line. Target landscape: Two or three suspects: one accused by independent, partly corroborating night claims (watch report, trailseer follow, visitor list, investigator read) who denies and counter-accuses, versus the accuser(s), who are unverified but consistent with each other. Evidence outweighs behavior on the accused, and nobody is confirmed by the Game Master.
  *Do:* On a day-vote with low heat, vote with the evidence-backed majority on the player whose story is contradicted by independent night reports (a watch claim, a follow match, a visitor count, a counter-claim) rather than on mood. If you have not yet picked, ask for a Trailseer or Sentinel to corroborate before the vote, then vote with the room. Each eviction of a wolf or lone killer moves Town toward winning, which is what your Town pick needs.

Stakes: With wolves or the lone killer nearly gone, one more correct elimination can end or nearly end the game; the vote is rarely decisive alone but it fixes the board toward a Town win, which is the side the speculator pick favors. Consensus: Largely aligned: a pack of accusers forms on matching tracking and check reports plus the reveals of dead wolves. My position: With the majority, joining the pile-on early or at the last minute rather than holding out. Heat: Low to moderate: no accusation round is aimed at you, though the accused may have counter-accused or voted against you. Target landscape: One accused player named by matching independent tracking or check claims (trailseer, sentinel, investigator); the claims are unverified but agree with each other and with confirmed reveals. The alternative candidates have no case against them; the accused's defense is blanket denial or counter-accusation.
  *Do:* On a day-3 vote where several tracking or check reports (trailseer, sentinel, investigator) converge on one accused and confirmed wolves are already known, vote with that convergent group for the accused instead of an unrelated player. Removing a convergent target sharpens the endgame toward Town, which pays off a Town pick; if you still hold your pick, the converging reports are your cue to lock in the side that now looks to be winning.
- key c296637d-c496-45de-9fda-e3800c55c153 (score 0.728) *When:* Day 2 first vote: a claimed power role admitted their reported result looked wrong and the whole table is accusing them, while you have not yet made your side pick. Stakes: Early elimination of a Town role weakens Town's information while the serial killer and wolves are still active; a mistaken unanimous vote is hard to reverse and your own pick depends on Town surviving. Target landscape: One claimed Sentinel under real pressure after reporting a watch result that omitted their own visit; the other claimant (a trailseer) looks consistent.   *Do:* When the table is piling onto a single claimed role over a stated inconsistency and there is no stronger lead, vote with the consensus only after checking that the inconsistency is a real rules violation rather than a misstatement. ## Lookup 7: luna game db66b748, player_7 the fortune_teller, night_action, day 2

**Situation the seat wrote:** After the village abstained on day 2, no one was eliminated and the vote record contains no decisive consensus. At night, the wolves and lone killer can each attack; the public Sentinel claimant may be exposed, but there is no confirmed target. I have 0 points and two self-bets remaining, so the decision is between predicting another player's death and using a self-bet that would make me immune to attacks but earn no points. Information landscape: Information-starved: the Investigator and Trailseer were revealed dead, and the Sentinel claim and visitor result are unverified. There is no public attack pattern or confirmed role evidence pointing to tonight's victim; any victim or role prediction is uncertain. Stakes: With 8 alive and 4 eliminations until the leading evil faction can win, tonight's death does not make the game immediately endable by one more elimination. A correct death bet advances my separate point goal, while a self-bet trades that chance for protection; the faction balance is not on a one-action knife edge. Target landscape: The exposed player claiming Sentinel is a plausible night target because of the public claim, but that role is unverified. Other living players have no confirmed roles from the public record; there is no clear evidence identifying a likely victim or their role. Betting on myself would prevent an attack on me but score no points that night.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key c61b65f7-5dad-407e-8216-a617b23365a3 (score 0.81) *When:* Midgame with one point scored and one more needed. The wolves and the Necromancer are killing nightly, a double attack on the sentinel and the investigator's death show their focus, and your earlier bet on a non-targeted player missed. Information landscape: Information-richer: revealed illusionist, trailseer record, necromancer vote-out, and a public attack on the sentinel. The killers' next preference is unstated, so the pick is inference from the kill pattern. Stakes: You need one more point to win and each wasted night costs a bet, while both self-bets remain unused. Missed bets on a player nobody is attacking waste nights that cannot be recovered. Target landscape: Six to seven living players: public-activity roles (sentinel, healer) that the wolves or a reanimating killer are visibly pressing, versus a quiet vote-aligned accuser with no sign of being targeted. The basis is public kill patterns and attack reports, not hidden role knowledge.
  *Do:* When you need a point and the previous night's kill pattern shows whom the killers are pressing (the attacked sentinel, the healer, or a role they just struck at), bet on that visible target to die rather than on a quiet accuser nobody is attacking. If your last bet missed, change the pick to follow the killers' current focus instead of repeating it, and keep your self-bets in hand until you have a reason to use them.

No attack on a night-immune player has identified the Serial Killer, and no such probe is known to have been used.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key ad54351b-e263-41b7-94cd-42228b5eb242 (score 0.839) *When:* Night 2 after a tied day-2 vote with no elimination. The pack kills a scrutinized or quiet player, the illusionist will conceal the body, and the chanteuse must pick which of several active town information roles to cancel. The pack's illusionist is already tied to a visit or is exposed by a claim. Information landscape: Moderately informative but unverified. There are conflicting role claims, a trailseer and sentinel who have reported, and a tied vote. Hard confirmation exists only for any dead roles. The chanteuse does not know every hidden actor, such as a necromancer using a dead body. Stakes: Mid-game with several town information roles alive. One unblocked witness to the illusionist's visit can cost the pack a wolf and bring the pack's win condition much further away, so which witness gets blocked matters more than whether a block happens. Forward exposure: The block is private and can change every night, but a blocked player can say so publicly and name their intended target, which hands town a corroborating signal. An unblocked watcher's report then stands alone. Once the night resolves the choice cannot be undone, so a wrong pick leaves the illusionist's visit open to whoever you left free. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed information roles: a trailseer and a sentinel who reported night-1 results, a claimed healer, and unconfirmed sentinel, sigilist and healer slots. Nobody's claim is verified. The trailseer was the loudest threat to the pack, but the sentinel's watch on the victim was the result that actually exposed the illusionist.
  *Do:* When the pack is killing a victim and the illusionist will visit that body to conceal it, spend the block on the information role most likely to see that visit rather than defaulting to the trailseer. Block the sentinel if it has publicly used a watch and the victim is the kind of player it would watch (a scrutinized or quiet player). Block the trailseer only if it has already followed or named the illusionist. Hiding the body hides only the carrier's visit, so the illusionist's own visit stays visible to a watcher on the victim, or to a follower of the illusionist, unless the illusionist carried the kill itself.
- key 867e7d60-4ebd-4fc2-95f5-af33f8bd61f9 (score 0.805) *When:* The pack has already settled its kill target and the chanteuse must choose a block target from the other town information roles, choosing between blocking the victim and blocking a different threat. Information landscape: Information-rich after day-two role claims, with public claims from the investigator, trailseer, and sigilist but no verified results. Stakes: Doubling the block on the kill target wastes the pack's single disruption and leaves another information role free to find a wolf. Spreading disruption keeps town results thin while the kill proceeds. Forward exposure: A block on a second player is a private visit that is only exposed if a Sentinel or Trailseer covers that player. Choosing the wrong player only wastes one night's block, and it can be changed next night. Target landscape: The kill target is the claimed investigator or trailseer. The block candidates are the other claimed information roles (a trailseer, sigilist, or investigator) and the suspected healer. The claims are public but unverified, so the choice rests on behavior.
  *Do:* When the pack kill is already aimed at one claimed role, use the roleblock on a different active threat, such as a claimed trailseer or an unresolved investigator, and not on the kill target. Pick the block target by which result would most expose the wolves. Never double up on the victim, because the kill itself already removes them.
- key 3da7fa03-c26a-4152-87d2-172883311cdc (score 0.799) *When:* Midgame after a daytime elimination, with several town power roles publicly claimed. The Trailseer has incriminated a wolf partner or promised an overnight follow, and the Sentinel is confirmed or active. The wolves must choose whom to kill and whom to block. Information landscape: Information-rich: wills of dead roles are revealed, a Trailseer visit chain is public, a Sentinel has been verified, and a Vigilante has claimed. Stakes: The board is still a few eliminations from wolf parity, so a clean night that removes or neutralises the investigative claimants can keep the pack alive, while a wasted block leaves the claimants free to expose a wolf. Forward exposure: A block leaves no public record and the target alone learns they were roleblocked, so the move stays hidden if they die and is only a mild tell if they survive. The block is a visit, so a watcher or follower on you could see it, and it cannot be undone once placed. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed Trailseer, the confirmed or claimed Sentinel, a claimed Vigilante and quiet unconfirmed players. The claims rest on partial verification (a Sentinel catch, a visit chain), not on hard proof.
  *Do:* When town has publicly claimed active investigative roles (Trailseer, Sentinel, Investigator) and you are in an information-rich midgame, aim the roleblock at the claimant most likely to act on the pack, and coordinate with your partner so the kill hits the other claimant or a high-threat role. If the kill is going to land on the claimant you would block, block the other active claimant, because a victim who dies makes the block redundant.
- key ac2dc81c-bdc9-4925-970d-d7f644b8f56a (score 0.795) *When:* Night 2 after an abstain-heavy or one-sided day vote. The pack has chosen its kill, and the chanteuse must pick which town night action to cancel while claimed visit-readers could expose the pack. Information landscape: Moderately informed. There are public claims of visit-based roles, a few confirmed reveals or wills, and no hard evidence against the wolves. Claims are unverified but their roles are plausible. Stakes: Early game with town still numerous and no near parity. One watch or follow report that names a pack member can swing the next day's vote, so removing the visit-readers' information is worth more than a speculative block. Forward exposure: The block is private, but the blocked player is told and may announce a roleblock the next day, which shows that a Chanteuse exists without pointing at you. Blocking is a visit, so a watcher or tracker who is not blocked can still see it. The choice is reversible every night. Target landscape: Claimed Sentinel and Trailseer have public information-role claims backed by visit reports or a dead Sentinel's will. Unclaimed quiet or questioning players are judged on behavior only. The kill target is often the claimed watcher, whom a healer may save.
  *Do:* On night 2, block the claimed Sentinel or Trailseer whose watch or follow could cover the pack's kill or your own visit, and above all a claimed watcher who is the kill target. If both claim, block the one your pack's visits would pass in front of, and change the block target on later nights so you are not a repeated pattern.

## Lookup 24: haiku game dcb8016a, player_9 the investigator, night_action, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 sentinel, player_3 illusionist, player_4 sigilist, player_5 chanteuse, player_6 speculator, player_7 necromancer, player_8 vigilante, player_9 investigator, player_10 trailseer

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 2 ended in a forced abstain with no elimination after a single player pushed a lone elimination vote against player_8. As the investigator holding two unrevealed checks, you must decide whether to spend a check on a player tonight, while the lone accuser's motive is unexplained and the rest of the table stayed silent. Information landscape: Information-starved: no claims, no confirmed night results, no will read out, and only the vote record exists. The investigator's own private checks are the only hard information in the game. Stakes: Ten alive with six eliminations needed before the leading evil faction can win, so no single check or vote can end the game next turn; the cost of a wasted check is significant because only two remain and the information base is empty, but the board can absorb an early misread. Target landscape: Nine living candidates other than yourself (player_1 through player_8 and player_10); none has a public role claim or confirmed result. The only public signal is the day 2 vote, where everyone abstained except one player who voted to eliminate player_8, a lone accusation with no stated reason on record.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key fcee63b9-c88d-4d85-ad1c-766fc5ef79f9 (score 0.859) *When:* Day 2 ended with a near-universal abstain vote and you stand out as the sole non-abstainer or a revealed investigator. You face a night where you may be hit by a wolf kill and must decide whether to spend a check or hold both. Information landscape: Information-starved: no deaths or role claims, only the day-2 vote tally showing abstentions and one or two outliers. No hard leads on alignment. Stakes: A night death erases that night's check result and the unspent check with it. Spending a check early gives the best chance of a usable Suspicious result before the wolves cut you down. Holding both checks risks dying with them unused. Target landscape: Nine living players: one vocal player you already voted against, and several quiet players blending into the abstain consensus. None has a confirmed role or any evidence against them. Selection is by behavioral standout or blending.
  *Do:* If you have two checks and are likely to be attacked soon (your role is known, or you stood out by voting alone), spend one now on the most behaviorally anomalous player instead of holding both. A check you never use is worth nothing if you die. Pick the target on a concrete behavioral signal such as an outlier vote or a conspicuous silence, not at random.
- key f03986da-a81b-428f-852b-96413a396132 (score 0.849) *When:* Day 2 ended in near-unanimous abstention or no elimination, with one or two players breaking consensus. The investigator is choosing where to spend a check before a wolf kill or roleblock can stop them. Information landscape: Thin evidence: a night-1 death, perhaps one unverified claim, and a voting record showing only a few non-abstainers. Mostly behavioral reads, with few or no confirmed alignments. Stakes: Wolves are not yet near parity, but the investigator is a prime kill target and may not survive many nights. Each remaining check is scarce, so spending it on the most informative anomaly matters. Target landscape: A handful of living players with no confirmed roles. One or two stand out by breaking the abstain consensus, casting a lone vote, or voting against the investigator. The rest are quiet abstainers with no trail, so the choice rests on voting behavior rather than hard evidence.
  *Do:* After a no-elimination Day 2, spend a check on the player whose public behavior stands out, such as the sole proactive voter, a lone dissenter against the room, or a member of a bloc that voted against you. If the result is Suspicious, you have a concrete wolf lead. If it is Not suspicious, you have cleared a likely power-role ally, though that read does not rule out a lone killer.
- key 549afc3b-ce66-4252-be08-fd8a0983b073 (score 0.824) *When:* Night 2 after an early town-role death and an abstain vote. A key info-role claim, or a result that could steer the next vote, sits unverified, and the investigator has one check left. Information landscape: Mostly information-starved. The only solid information is the investigator's own earlier read, and the other claims are unverified. Stakes: The town needs one trustworthy lead to find the killers. Checking the claimant whose uncorroborated result matters most can turn the best available lead into something the town can act on, without wasting the last check. Target landscape: Eight living players. One player has a claim or result with real consequences for the vote, but no one else can confirm it. The other candidates are silent or unclaimed. Basis is the claim's importance, not evidence against anyone.
  *Do:* If a pivotal claimant, such as a sentinel or trailseer, has an unverified result that the town may act on, use the check on that claimant. A Not suspicious read adds weight to a lead the town would otherwise have to take on faith, and a Suspicious read exposes a false claim. Skip claimants whose visit is already explained by a corroborating watch or follow.

Stakes: Mid-game board where a single wasted or rogue vote does little to the count, but drawing a night kill before scoring two points ends your win condition. Staying unnoticed matters more than being first to guess right.
