True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 sigilist, player_4 speculator, player_5 illusionist, player_6 vigilante, player_7 chanteuse, player_8 trailseer, player_9 investigator, player_10 sentinel

**Situation the seat wrote:** The day-2 vote ended in an abstention with no elimination: nearly everyone abstained, while one player voted to eliminate the player whom an unverified Investigator claim had cleared as Not suspicious. The Investigator’s claimed result, your private trail result on a different player, and an unverified Sentinel account provide limited, non-alignment evidence as the next trail decision approaches. Information landscape: Information-starved for alignment: there are no publicly confirmed roles or revealed wills, and the concealed night-1 victim’s role remains unknown. Available evidence consists of unverified role and result claims, your private finding that your target visited no one, and the public vote record; the corroborated visit route does not establish alignment. Stakes: With 9 players alive and 5 eliminations until the leading evil faction could win at worst for the town, a single result cannot end the game or flip which faction is winning; the concealed body’s unknown side means this is the closest possible parity estimate. Target landscape: The unresolved candidates are the player with a claimed Not-suspicious result and the player they checked, plus the player who alone voted to eliminate that checked player. The Investigator and Sentinel claims remain unverified; your own follow confirms only that the watched player visited no one, not anyone’s alignment.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 2e1b9a39-43b2-4d0b-84f8-00983c84b1bd (score 0.791) *When:* Night 2 after a night-1 death and an all-abstain day-2 vote. Nobody has claimed a night result, and the Trailseer must choose whom to follow with only discussion tone to go on. Information landscape: Information-starved: one night death, a unanimous abstain, a few cautious statements, no night claims and no cross-checkable roles. Stakes: Mid-game with several evil factions alive and no eliminations yet from votes. One follow can give the town its first concrete lead, but a misread visit could point at an innocent, so the result needs corroboration. Target landscape: Living players with the most public presence in the day-2 discussion, none with a confirmed role or hard evidence against them. Picks rest on behavior (who urged caution or steered the room), not on evidence. A follow only shows where the target went, not why.
  *Do:* On a night 2 with no claims, no hard leads and an all-abstain day, follow a vocal, influential player whose day talk steered the room, rather than a quiet player or a past victim. If the follow shows a visit, treat it as one data point that names a possible partner or victim, not as proof, and weigh the visited player's own record before acting on it.
- key 6d3bf910-2e7f-4bde-8de2-4ea04eeb8cc9 (score 0.751) *When:* After a day elimination validated an investigative claim, the Trailseer chooses between auditing that claimant and chasing unknown suspects. The claimant may be a target for the wolves or the Chanteuse's block. Information landscape: Information-rich. There is a confirmed wolf flip from the Investigator's check, public claims, and a Sentinel watch that reported no visitors. Stakes: The remaining evil, a wolf and the lone killer, is still unknown. The Investigator's survival and credibility drive the village's votes, so verifying that they acted gives some value, but the follow adds no direct information on the remaining evil. Target landscape: A claimed Investigator whose check was validated by an elimination, versus a claimed Sentinel with a quiet watch result or unconfirmed quiet players. The choice rests on the value of verifying a key Town role.
  *Do:* When the Investigator's claim has just produced a confirmed result and the room trusts it, follow that Investigator to see whether they visited someone. If they visited no one, that matches a roleblock or a skipped check, but it is not proof of role. If they visited a player, you confirm they acted and learn who they checked.
- key e6f1b358-f883-456d-9c01-717103095445 (score 0.742) *When:* After a tied or no-result day 2, you are a claimed Trailseer with an unverified or contested result. Your night 1 or 2 follow was blocked, or your target is publicly known, and a Chanteuse may be blocking you on a predictable pattern. Information landscape: Information-starved: your own private follow result, a few unverified claims, and a split vote record. No confirmed wolf identity. Stakes: Each night of lost trail information costs the town its only movement data while the wolves keep carrying kills. A wasted follow, or your death, removes your role from the board. Target landscape: Several living players: the previously followed or publicly accused suspect, plus quiet unclaimed players, a claimed Investigator or Sigilist, and neutrals. All have little or no trail data, and the suspect was chosen on behavior rather than evidence.
  *Do:* If you were roleblocked last night, or you have publicly named your intended follow target, pick a different living player to follow tonight, ideally one you have not mentioned. A repeat on a publicly known target is easy for the Chanteuse to predict, and in this cluster those repeats were blocked and yielded no result.
- key ed5057e1-7e8f-4a63-9a36-01d0c99f206a (score 0.74) *When:* One or two nights in, a killer's victim has been found. Town's checks and watches are unverified. A Sentinel may be watching the same door, and you must decide whose outgoing movements to trail. Information landscape: Some evidence exists: a revealed night-1 victim, public vote and claim history, and visible activity from one claimed role. No hard confirmation of any killer's identity. Stakes: Town still has room to find the killers, but each night without usable, corroborated information lets the wolves and the lone killer close in. A result that another role's report can corroborate is worth more than a lone claim. Target landscape: A self-claimed, visibly active Town role (such as a Sigilist) who is a likely kill target, versus unclaimed or unverified players and a publicly accused suspect. The choice rests on predicting which player the killers will go after, not on hard evidence.
  *Do:* Follow an exposed, likely-to-be-attacked player, such as a claimed Sigilist or Investigator who is active and already drawing attention, instead of the loudest suspect. You learn whom that player visited, and your own visit to their door can be recorded by a Sentinel watching them. That gives the town an independent cross-check on your claim.

## Lookup 1014: haiku game 20497d9e, player_10 the sigilist, night_action, day 3
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 trailseer, player_4 sentinel, player_5 fortune_teller, player_6 chanteuse, player_7 investigator, player_8 vigilante, player_9 illusionist, player_10 sigilist

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 3 ended with the healer lynched, and the village is now split between a trailseer claim and players who deny it, with the carrier of the wolves' kill unknown. The sigilist must predict which living player will attack tonight, since a sigil placed on a player who does not attack is wasted, and a sigil on a carrier or on a vigilante who shoots strikes them. Information landscape: Information-starved: no living player has verified a night result, the sentinel and investigator and fortune teller and healer are dead, and the only public data are the sentinel's will (watched player_9, saw no one) and the healer's will (protected player_4 and player_6, no attacks announced) plus one unverified trailseer chain. Stakes: Six alive with two eliminations needed before the leading evil faction can win, and the game cannot end within one more elimination, so a single misplaced sigil costs one of two remaining sigils without ending the game but narrowing the margin for error; a wasted sigil leaves the sigilist with one strike for the rest of the game. Forward exposure: Placing a sigil is a visible-in-outcome act: if it strikes, the struck player is revealed as an attacker, and a sigil with no effect narrows the target to a non-attacker; the sigil itself is spent on placement whether or not it triggers, so the choice is irreversible. Target landscape: Six players alive: the sigilist's own seat plus five others (player_2, player_3, player_6, player_8, player_9). Public roles are unclaimed except a trailseer claim by player_3 with a night chain that has not been verified; the vigilante has not yet revealed. Evidence is behavioral and vote-based, with the trailseer chain the only concrete trail and it places the now-lynched healer, not any living player, at the sentinel.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 89606edf-43f9-4c73-bfa3-5cc1f21c0e00 (score 0.866) *When:* Late-game night with one wolf or lone killer still active among five or six living players, and the sigilist is a likely kill target. Information landscape: Information-rich on dead evil roles, cleared players and voting records, but the last attacker is identified only by behavior and elimination. Stakes: Each night costs a player and the board is close to parity, so a sigil that strikes the last killer can win the game outright, and one held until death is wasted. Forward exposure: Placing a sigil is a secret visit that spends the charge whether or not it triggers, so it cannot be undone. A no-effect result means the target did not attack, which fits a non-killer or an attacker who held back, and it does not clear them. Target landscape: A few living unverified candidates, with the others cleared by the investigator or sentinel. The best suspect is picked from behavior, such as an isolated vote against a confirmed role or a quiet profile, because no hard proof exists.
  *Do:* When few players remain (about 5-6) and one killer is left, place a sigil each night on the most probable unverified killer, chosen from voting record and investigator or sentinel clearances, rather than holding both sigils. If that player attacks, the sigil strikes them, so you convert a read into a kill. With one sigil left, spend it on your best read, since an unspent sigil does nothing if you are killed first.
- key 76f5f000-6264-4a96-9412-e691d6076356 (score 0.858) *When:* One or both wolves may already be dead. A killer keeps targeting a known town player, and the sigilist is tempted to put the sigil on that expected victim to counter the next attack. Information landscape: Role information is public, such as confirmed investigator results and verified healer saves. There is still no trace pointing at the remaining killer. Stakes: The remaining killer can keep striking, but the town is not on the brink. Preserving limited sigils for an identified attacker matters more than acting every night. Forward exposure: Holding commits you to nothing and reveals nothing. You keep both charges and can place one on any later night. Target landscape: The remaining candidates are likely victims, such as a claimed investigator who survived an attack or was healer-protected, or unvetted players with no attack evidence. None has been tied to a kill.
  *Do:* If no living suspect has evidence of attacking and the only candidates are likely victims or confirmed-Town players such as a protected investigator, keep your sigils that night. Wait for a suspect with an attack pattern. A sigil on a player who does not attack does nothing, and a likely victim is the one player who will not attack.
- key 8b96bf95-3f3f-44cb-9330-0ed1c97c5599 (score 0.857) *When:* Late game (night 3) after one evil was voted out. At least one wolf is believed to still be alive and attacking every night. You have not yet used either sigil and have already counter-claimed the sigilist role publicly. Information landscape: Mixed: in one case rich (revealed roles, visitor lists, an investigator's clearing read), in the other thin, with unverified claims and only the fact that attacks were happening each night. Either way the sigil depends on predicting who will attack. Stakes: A few players remain and a wolf is still killing each night. Removing the last wolf with a sigil strike can end the game or leave only the serial killer for a day vote. Holding both sigils unused wastes the one tool that punishes a night attacker. Forward exposure: Placing the sigil is a visit and spends it whether or not it triggers. A strike is announced publicly as a sigil death, which exposes you as the sigilist, but your role was already claimed publicly, so little extra exposure. It cannot be undone once placed, though a second sigil remains. Target landscape: A few living candidates, including a contested healer claimant versus a player cleared Not suspicious by the investigator, or an unresolved field of vigilante, trailseer, illusionist, speculator, sentinel and serial killer. The best-supported target is the one public deduction marks as an evil attacker; where the evidence is thin, the pick rests on a guess about who attacks.
  *Do:* On a night when only one or two attackers likely remain (a lone wolf, or a wolf plus a killer) and you hold both sigils, place a sigil on the player you most suspect of attacking, rather than keeping both. A sigil strikes only if the target attacks, so choose the likeliest attacker from public reads: a wolf cleared of nothing, a contested claimant, or whoever the room's math points to. If you place the sigil and it does not strike, you still have one left for the next night.

## Lookup 1015: luna game db66b748, player_9 the illusionist, day_vote, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 vigilante, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 sentinel, player_4 sigilist, player_5 healer, player_6 investigator, player_7 fortune_teller, player_8 trailseer, player_9 illusionist, player_10 chanteuse

**Situation the seat wrote:** After Night 1 killed the publicly revealed Investigator and Trailseer, an unverified Sentinel claim that the Trailseer visited the watched player prompted questions about whether that visit implicates the watched player. The Trailseer’s role makes following a plausible explanation, and the discussion has not produced a concrete accusation or vote direction. Information landscape: Information is limited: two Town roles were publicly revealed dead, but neither death report supplies a visitor trace. The only claimed Night 1 result is the Sentinel’s watch showing the Trailseer visited the watched player; no independent result corroborates it, and no one has presented a verified lead identifying the killer. Stakes: Eight players are alive and four eliminations remain until the leading evil faction can win; this decision cannot end the game within one more elimination, so the immediate stakes are not a one-result swing. Consensus: There is no clear village consensus or coordinated accusation. The speakers so far agree that the Trailseer visiting the watched player does not identify who attacked the Trailseer, and they are seeking more information. My position: Not driving a consensus; your stated interpretation is aligned with the cautious public reading, while the room has no clear direction. Heat: Low: the Sentinel asked why you were watched, but no one has accused you or cited evidence against you. Forward exposure: Saying the Trailseer’s visit is consistent with a follow action, and asking why the Sentinel chose that target, would reinforce a public interpretation already voiced without revealing your role or night knowledge. It is readily reversible as discussion, though a later shift to accusing the watched player could look inconsistent. Target landscape: No elimination candidates have emerged. The watched player is not publicly implicated by the visit alone; the Sentinel is an unverified claimant, and the Trailseer’s revealed role makes a follow action a plausible explanation. No evidence currently distinguishes other living players as vote targets. Public vs private: Privately, you know the pack killed the player revealed as the Investigator. Publicly, both the Investigator and Trailseer were revealed among the Night 1 dead, and the Sentinel claim says the Trailseer visited the player they watched; the room has not connected that visit to the attack.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key e706b93b-14bf-42ad-9627-5b5e4d295384 (score 0.679) *When:* Day 2 vote: I am the primary target after an accuser's check or watch result, backed by a second claimant. My own counterclaim or deflection has attracted no followers. Information landscape: Starved to moderate: conflicting or unverified role claims, a concealed or ambiguous body, and a visit report that implicates me. No role reveals confirm either side. Stakes: My single vote cannot change an 8-1 outcome, so its value lies in the signal it sends and in not tying myself to a doomed counterclaim. Losing a wolf this early also removes a voter and a conceal that the pack cannot replace. Consensus: Lopsided against me, resting on a corroborating claim from a second player and a weak defense from me. My position: Holding out alone against the forming consensus. Heat: Very high: several players named me, I was the most accused, and I gave a last word. Forward exposure: A lone vote on the accuser is public and permanent, ties me to my counterclaim and reads as self-protective. If I am eliminated, my role reveal confirms the accuser and makes them trusted for later days. Target landscape: Candidates are the accuser (a claimed investigator or sentinel with partial corroboration) and myself. Neither can be verified by a role reveal yet, so the room weighs the claims and my defense. Public vs private: I privately know the accuser's read on me is true, or that the visit evidence against me is accurate. Publicly the village sees a corroborated accusation against me and an unresolved counter-claim from me, with no confirmed roles.
  *Do:* When the room is lopsided against you on corroborated evidence, check whether any accuser's claim has real traction before you vote. If the room is clearly locked on you, put your vote on a second-tier suspect the room already doubts, not the accuser who named you. That pairs your vote with other doubt, avoids another lone self-protective vote, and keeps your last word credible if it reads as a sincere read.
- key 55fb9d61-6647-4904-9fb4-0c459f083dac (score 0.675) *When:* A Town information role has publicly tied you to a night kill, and a second player backs the claim. Your partner is alive but is distancing from you, and you are the favorite to be eliminated today. Information landscape: Moderately rich: a specific result naming you, corroborating claims and a prior vote record. All of it is unverified, but it fits together. Stakes: Losing you leaves one wolf with several eliminations still to go before parity. Your vote cannot rescue you, so its only use is to hit the most dangerous accurser and keep the surviving wolf alive. Consensus: The village is aligned against you on a consistent claim and its corroboration, and it has little other evidence to weigh. My position: Holding out alone with a counter-vote while the room piles onto you. Heat: High. You are the named suspect and the primary target of the day, with several players accusing you from the start. Forward exposure: Your vote and denial go on the public record and cannot be retracted. If the accuser is read as truthful and you die, your role is revealed and your partner is left to survive alone. Target landscape: The candidates are the accuser and the player who corroborates them. Neither has a confirmed role, and the case rests on a specific claim plus consistent backing rather than on behavior alone. Public vs private: You privately know the accusation against you is true, for example the watch result that names you at a victim's door. Publicly it is only an unverified claim, though corroborating claims and a roleblock story make it look consistent.
  *Do:* When the vote is converging on you and your single vote cannot change the count, spend it on the accuser whose claim or case against you is most credible. That is the Town player most likely to hold accurate information, and removing them helps your partner survive. Keep denying the specific claim instead of switching to a new target, so your partner can still look like an independent Town voter.
- key f9bd93ed-0b76-456c-845c-45c3709edbae (score 0.66) *When:* A day 2 vote in which two players claim investigator, your wolf partner is one of them (a fake claim) and was placed at a night scene by a watch. The village must choose between them. Information landscape: Moderate: one watch result and one follow result were shared, but both investigator claims are unverified and the key check result was never given. Stakes: The vote decides whether the town keeps its checker or the pack's partner is exposed. Eight players are alive and parity is about three eliminations away, so removing the real checker materially helps the wolves. Consensus: A clear majority is forming against the claimed investigator on the basis of claim contradiction and your partner's push. My position: With the majority, not driving. Heat: Low: you have barely spoken, are not named in the accusations, and stand outside the two-way fight. Forward exposure: A vote for the claimed investigator matches your partner's push and the room's lean, so it exposes little. Voting to save your partner would tie you to a player the visit evidence already places at the scene; the vote is public and permanent either way. Target landscape: Two candidates in a role-claim contradiction: a claimed investigator with no verifiable result, and your partner, who gave a counter-claim and was placed at a night scene by a watch. Neither role is confirmed; the basis is claim contradiction and a visit report. Public vs private: Privately you know your partner is a wolf and the investigator claimant is probably the real role; publicly the room sees only a contradiction between two role claims.
  *Do:* When a claimed investigator or other checker is contested and your partner has pushed against them, join the majority vote to eliminate that player, provided your partner is not the one the room is leaning toward. This removes a role that threatens the pack, while a vote with the room and your partner's push keeps your heat low; if the majority is instead forming on your partner, avoid defending them loudly and keep your vote on the room's lead alternative.

Stakes: Nine alive with five eliminations until the leading evil faction can win, so no single night or vote ends the game; a wrong bet costs one point's progress toward two, and a self-bet spent without a real threat wastes one of the two remaining uses that protect the scoring path. Public roles: only player_2 (trailseer) is confirmed dead; no living player has a public role confirmation. Claims are unverified: nobody has announced a checked or watched result, and the day-2 vote split (abstain plurality, two players each accused by one voter) gives no hard evidence on any target.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key b40602be-b1e0-4d1d-a39c-a4c14a8a34d2 (score 0.847) *When:* Late-midgame night where the killers' obvious priority target, a verified or claimed investigator, has been publicly shielded or saved by the healer, while other prominent claimants are unprotected. The Fortune Teller must choose a bet to reach two points. Information landscape: Information-rich: both wolves or the serial killer are dead or confirmed, healer saves are announced, and protection patterns are visible to every player, including the killers. Stakes: Scoring needs two points, and each zero-point night shrinks the remaining nights in which they can be earned. Repeating a bet the healer can block yields no points and pushes the Fortune Teller toward riskier late bets. Target landscape: A claimed investigator or other focal Town player who is openly or recently healer-protected; an unprotected claimed healer or trailseer; and quieter unverified players. The evidence is public announcements of saves and claims, not behavior.
  *Do:* When the obvious high-profile target was publicly healer-protected or saved last night, bet on a different likely victim instead, such as an unprotected prominent claimant (the claimed healer, or a trailseer or sentinel with no cover). If no protector remains alive, bet on the most prominent unprotected Town claimant.
- key 93d3a7dc-3f0e-41a2-96ff-cd3fa541c7ce (score 0.836) *When:* Night 2 after a no-elimination or abstain day. One claimant of a night-info role is the focal point of the discussion and votes, so the wolves and the lone killer both have reason to kill them. Information landscape: Moderately informative. There is a public role claim and a vote record concentrated on one player, plus evidence from the earlier night that killers are active. Nothing is verified, and the read rests on the claimant's visibility and not on confirmed roles. Stakes: Scoring two points here ends the Fortune Teller's win condition outright. A miss costs nothing but a night, though only a few nights remain to score. Target landscape: Eight or nine living players. One stands out as a visible information threat: a self-declared Trailseer or Sentinel who was roleblocked or took the day's votes. Their role is unverified and rests on their own claim. The rest are quiet, with no comparable profile.
  *Do:* When a player has drawn real visibility, such as a self-declared info-role claim (Trailseer, Sentinel) that has also taken votes or a roleblock, bet on that player to die and name the claimed role. Killers have a motive to remove them, and a correct role is worth two points while a wrong role still scores one for the death.
- key f26d1942-ed34-4eef-8c30-4cb94479142d (score 0.824) *When:* After a day in which the Fortune Teller cast an isolated dissenting vote or otherwise stands out, the remaining killers must choose a victim. The obvious saved target is likely covered again, which makes the exposed Fortune Teller the easy kill. Information landscape: Information-rich: the public voting record and announced saves expose who dissented and who the healer protected, and the killers can read both. Stakes: If the Fortune Teller dies with fewer than two points it loses permanently. A self-bet makes an attack on it fail, so it survives to bet again, though it scores nothing that night. Target landscape: The Fortune Teller's own safety, via a self-bet, versus betting on a re-targeted saved claimant or another Town player. The killers may bypass the healer-protected claimant and hit the visible outlier instead.
  *Do:* When your day-3 vote or behavior marks you as an outlier or likely target, and you are not close to the second point, use one of your two self-bets instead of betting on another player. The attack on you fails that night, so you stay alive to score later.
- key 57f8fb12-a292-4058-8012-6de9fc026a47 (score 0.786) *When:* Mid-game night (night 2-3) after a wolf was voted out or a tie vote. Claims are public, and a Necromancer, wolves or a Serial Killer may strike. The Fortune Teller is not under suspicion and needs two points to win. Information landscape: Moderate public signals: claimed roles, announced attacker types (reanimated attack), prior deaths and vote records. Nothing confirms who the killers will choose. Stakes: The game is not at the brink, but each night is a scoring chance and the Fortune Teller needs two points. A night with several possible killers gives more than one death to land on, so a well-chosen bet is worth making every night. Target landscape: Several living claimants (a Healer who revealed whom they protected, a twice-attacked Investigator, a Sentinel or Trailseer) and contested players from a claim clash or tie vote. Roles are public claims, unverified. Picks rest on behavior and exposure, not confirmed evidence.
  *Do:* Each night, bet on the player the killers are most likely to hit, judged from public exposure: a freshly claimed Healer or information role, a player at the center of a claim clash, or one just named by an announced reanimated attack. Add the role guess only when a public claim makes it a strong inference, since a right role is worth two points and a wrong role still scores one for the death.
- key 5e89a988-13eb-4cb0-8a5f-e8afb06ca6bf (score 0.781) *When:* Early game, night 2. Wolves and a lone killer are probably all hunting, and the town has just produced a visible claimed power role. You must bet on who dies while the healer may protect the most obvious target. Information landscape: Information-starved. There is one revealed death, an unverified claim or two, and little or no vote record. Nothing shows who the killers will pick beyond how visible the claimant is. Stakes: Early game with no imminent parity. The bet is a modest scoring chance, since a miss costs one night's attempt toward the two points. The two self-bets stay in reserve. Target landscape: Seven to nine living players: one or two public power-role claimants (a trailseer, sigilist or sentinel claimant who stands out as a likely kill target), plus quiet unclaimed players with no behavioral signal. The healer is alive and unrevealed. The only evidence is who died on night 1 and who claimed since.
  *Do:* On an early night with both self-bets still held, when killers are active and one publicly claimed investigative role stands out, bet on that claimant to die and name their claimed role only if you would accept losing the second point. Accept that a healer save can turn an accurate prediction into zero, since only a death scores. Do not move your bet to a quiet unclaimed player, because the evidence points at the claimant.

The living players' roles remain unverified claims. Stakes: Mid-game with several players alive, so a single block rarely flips the game, but the necromancer is being voted against and a wasted night or a visible trace costs tempo it cannot spare. Forward exposure: A block through the dead chanteuse's body shows the dead wolf's name at the door of any watching Sentinel, telling the town a Necromancer is active though not who. True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 trailseer, player_4 sentinel, player_5 fortune_teller, player_6 chanteuse, player_7 investigator, player_8 vigilante, player_9 illusionist, player_10 sigilist

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day three with two town roles and the Serial Killer's kills still unresolved: the investigator and sentinel are both dead, and the village has just passed two abstain votes while the Fortune Teller and a probing wolf-partner cover remain unrevealed. You must decide whether to keep abstaining or to lend a vote to a quiet player, knowing a vote record ties you to any target. Information landscape: Information-starved: no living claimed info role, no investigation or watch results on the record, only the dead sentinel's night-one watch which saw no one, and the day-one and day-two voting record. Stakes: Seven alive with three eliminations until the leading evil faction can win; a wrong vote is recoverable but each mis-elimination moves the wolves closer to parity, and the game cannot end within one more elimination. Consensus: The village has been abstaining as a bloc for two days with no hard leads; the room is aligned on not pushing thin evidence rather than on any target. My position: With the majority on abstaining, while your partner-linked wolf channel is pulling toward a quiet non-claimed target. Heat: Low on you right now: no accusation names you in the summary, but you have been the player steering toward quiet targets and asking for info-role claims, which a careful reader could find self-serving. Forward exposure: Any visible push toward a specific target or a claim would tie you to the vote record and to the wolf-channel kill choices; staying silent and abstaining keeps the record clean but leaves the village without info roles to lean on, and votes cannot be taken back once public. Target landscape: Seven living players with no public role claims; the only public vote record is a single vote by one player against the sentinel who was later killed by the pack and serial killer, so all remaining candidates are evidence-free behavioral reads only. Public vs private: Privately you are the Chanteuse, a wolf, and you know your pack partner the Illusionist is alive and unrevealed; publicly you are an unclaimed engaged town-seeming player, while the table reads the day as a quiet abstain-leaning stalemate with no claims on the board.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key a085d9d9-ad8e-4e4c-aa33-6323c724b0ea (score 0.811) *When:* After a night death, a claimed info role lays out a visit chain implicating another player. I appear only incidentally in that chain, and an evasive player asks me to share my information. Information landscape: Information-rich: a dead role's revealed will, a claimed multi-night Trailseer record, and other claims that are not yet verified. Stakes: Pack is within a couple of eliminations of parity. Letting the town spend its vote on the accused player, rather than on me or my partner, keeps the wolf bloc intact. Consensus: The room is aligned on testing the claimed visit chain and gathering around the player it implicates, with little attention on me. My position: With the majority, letting the accusation play out. Heat: Low: I was mentioned in passing, and the questioner's prompt did not frame me as an attacker. Forward exposure: Making no claim commits me to nothing and cannot be counter-claimed, and I can still claim later if pressure rises. The risk is that continued silence reads as evasive if another player keeps pressing me. Target landscape: The claimed info role, the player that role accuses, and any unproven claimant such as a Vigilante. The basis is claimed visit records and behavior, not confirmed results. Public vs private: Privately I am a wolf using night blocks, while publicly I read as an unaligned townie who was merely visited or mentioned.
  *Do:* When a player asks you to state your results and you are only a minor name in a claimed info role's visit chain, decline to counter-claim and instead call for the info roles to present testable, cross-checkable results. Let the room's attention settle on the player the visit chain actually accuses.
- key a32e8859-73cb-4356-92fe-9598f3cc6d4e (score 0.81) *When:* Late game with the wolf partner dead or revealed and the chanteuse as the last wolf. The town is debating announced night events, such as a vigilante shot, a claimed roleblock, or a concealed-role death, and accusing each other. Information landscape: Information-rich: confirmed role reveals, announced kills and saves, roleblock claims, and several town claims with public vote records. Factual gaps between what was announced and what a speaker says are easy to spot. Stakes: With about five alive and one wolf left, a single wrong vote or a consensus on the wolf ends the game. Keeping the room divided over other players preserves the wolf's path to parity. Consensus: The village is split and nobody has a clear majority target. Accusations run between other players and are driven by claims and roleblock or shot reports. My position: with majority Heat: Low to moderate. Nobody is naming the chanteuse. Any pressure would come from a slip about information the chanteuse should not have. Forward exposure: Citing only announced facts commits the chanteuse to nothing falsifiable and is easy to walk back. Citing an unannounced night event exposes insider knowledge, and the room can test it. Target landscape: Several town-claimed or accused players (speculator, sigilist, vigilante, a suspected serial killer, a quiet player) are under discussion. The case against each rests on claims and night reports, not on proof against the chanteuse. Public vs private: The chanteuse privately knows the pack's real night actions, kills and failed attacks, and which dead player was the partner. The town knows only the announced outcomes and what players claim.
  *Do:* When your wolf partner is already dead or revealed and you are the last wolf, speak only from facts the Game Master has publicly announced, such as confirmed shots, announced roleblocks, and revealed roles. Use them to point the room at the open questions about other players' claims, so suspicion spreads over several players instead of settling on you. Before you cite any night event, check that it was actually announced.

Information landscape: Information-rich: dead roles were revealed by the moderator, and living claimed information roles are publicly reporting night results. Stakes: The pack still has room before parity, but a wolf voted out now removes a killer and the chanteuse's block. Staying off the lurker list matters more than winning any argument. Consensus: The village is loosely aligned behind the claimed information roles' reports and a shared plan to press non-participants. My position: Holding out by staying silent, which is the position drawing the pressure. Heat: Moderate and rising, because the leaders have agreed to scrutinize players who stay silent after morning claims. Forward exposure: A short, non-committal contribution is cheap and reversible and commits to no role claim. Total silence is already public record once the room agrees to interrogate lurkers, and it invites a vote. Target landscape: Claimed investigator and claimed sentinel leading the discussion, the player they have cleared, and other uncommitted town members. The basis is mostly claimed night results plus behavior such as lurking. Public vs private: Publicly the room treats claimed information roles as leaders and flags silent players as suspect. Privately the chanteuse knows its partner's real status and what the pack did overnight, and cannot afford to be the quiet one.
  *Do:* When claimed information roles are reporting results and asking quiet players to engage, speak early with one concrete, town-sounding contribution. For example, ask a claimed role a specific question about its result, or give a short read on an uncommitted player who is not your partner. If your partner is under attack, keep the comment general and do not name or defend them.
- key ca82d9df-9537-424e-93c3-7fafa8bd729a (score 0.795) *When:* Day discussion after a concealed night kill in which the village focuses on your wolf partner and you are unscrutinized. If you say nothing, your partner is voted out and you are left alone. Information landscape: Fairly rich: an announced roleblock, a sigil placement with no effect, and a day-two trail pointing at your partner. That is enough for the town to build a case, so a counter-narrative needs a real hook. Stakes: Losing your partner now leaves a single wolf against a near-parity board, so one more elimination decides the game. Keeping your partner alive is worth a small amount of heat. Consensus: The village is converging fast on your partner, based on earlier tracking evidence and a fresh sigil or block result. My position: Holding out silently while the majority forms against your partner. Heat: Zero to low: nobody has brought you up or examined your earlier positions. Forward exposure: A brief, evidence-based doubt is easy to walk back and commits you to little. Heavy defense of your partner links you to them and is hard to undo. Target landscape: The main candidate is your partner, who has carryover suspicion from earlier tracking evidence. Other candidates are the Sigilist with a charge left and the roleblocked Sentinel, all claimed and unverified. Public vs private: Privately you know your partner is the last other wolf and you know why the night's events happened, for example that your block caused the Sentinel's failed watch. Publicly the village sees only an announced result and a suspect with carryover suspicion, and nobody is looking at you.
  *Do:* When the room converges on your partner and you are unscrutinized, offer at least one alternative suspect or a doubt about the evidence against your partner. Base it on real inconsistencies, such as a claimed result that can't be verified, and keep it short and low-heat. Don't stay completely silent, since silence lets the town unite on your partner and leaves you alone.

Information-starved on the remaining evil roles, because the only leads are vote splits or defense of the dead wolf. Stakes: Town is ahead but several evil players remain, and each night kill erodes the margin. A wasted bullet on a wrong target costs town a villager-side body. The only basis is voting behavior, not hard evidence, and no candidate is confirmed.
  *Do:* After a wolf is confirmed by a day elimination, with two bullets and no target backed by evidence, hold fire and keep both bullets. Shoot only when a night result, a checked Suspicious read, or a caught lie points at a specific player. Stakes: Several eliminations remain before wolves reach parity, so one night's patience costs little. Holding fire exposes nothing and keeps every option open. Target landscape: Seven to eight survivors. Losing the chanteuse, or drawing a sigil or investigator check by standing out, costs the wolves a blocker and leaves the partner alone. Consensus: The room is aligned on either abstaining or eliminating you, based on matching unverified claims about your night movement rather than on any announced fact. ## Lookup 2004: haiku game 20497d9e, player_2 the serial_killer, day_discussion, day 4
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 trailseer, player_4 sentinel, player_5 fortune_teller, player_6 chanteuse, player_7 investigator, player_8 vigilante, player_9 illusionist, player_10 sigilist

**Situation the seat wrote:** Four players remain after a day-3 lynch and a night-3 kill; the village has lost its healer, trailseer and sentinel and is now debating a claimed vigilante's accusations with no living information role confirmed. You, the lone killer, face the choice of which accuser to target next while keeping your public read clean. Information landscape: Information-starved: the only public records are the dead players' past night results and the day discussion; no living player has verifiable night information. Stakes: Four alive with the game able to end on the next elimination: at worst for the town, a single kill or vote could end the game, so each night decision carries a direct chance of ending the game. Consensus: The village has no settled consensus; the last vote eliminated a claimed healer and the remaining players are split on who is lying. My position: Holding out from any forming consensus, with no committed accusation. Heat: Low on you personally: no one has named you, and the accusations on day 3 were directed at the dead trailseer and the voted-out healer. Forward exposure: Any visible accusation or vote you make commits you to a public stance that is hard to retract; staying silent keeps your heat low but leaves the village's accusation pattern to form without you. Target landscape: Four living players: the claimed vigilante who has been accusing others, one quiet player, and two others with no public role claims; the only public role evidence is the accusation record and the trailseer's claimed chain, which you know is accurate about its night 1 and 2 visits. Public vs private: As the serial killer, you privately know you killed the fortune teller, sentinel and trailseer, while the village only knows a claimed trailseer was stabbed and a claimed vigilante (player_8) is alive; the public read is that the remaining players are still searching for the killer.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 84f2cc36-addf-4bee-b493-eae8086b8f70 (score 0.83) *When:* Late game (day 4-5) with the wolves already eliminated and 3-4 players alive. The town's surviving claims corroborate each other and the village is pointing at you as the only unexplained player. Information landscape: Information-rich for the town: revealed roles, cross-supporting claims and public vote records leave only one open slot. The serial killer has no evidence to offer in its own defense. Stakes: Every vote is decisive: being eliminated ends your win outright. With 4 alive you need two more deaths to win, so surviving the day by splitting or stalling the vote matters more than clearing your name. Consensus: The village is converging on one answer by elimination, since roles are revealed and the surviving claims support each other. The only unresolved name is yours. My position: Holding out alone against a forming majority, with at most one other player as a secondary target. Heat: High and rising: you are named or implied as the last unaccounted-for player, with no claim or counter-argument to deflect it. Forward exposure: Accusing the other unexplained player is a visible, committed vote target, but it needs no role claim and can be redirected as new reads arrive. Staying vague or silent commits you to nothing and gives the room nothing to doubt, so the elimination logic stands unopposed. Target landscape: The 2-3 other survivors, most with partial credentials: a trailseer or healer claim that cross-supports the other, a speculator cleared only by someone's follow report, or an unclaimed player. The basis is claims and elimination, not verified evidence, and you are the only one with no backing. Public vs private: Privately you are the Serial Killer and know you are not a wolf. Publicly you are simply the player the others have not yet explained, and the town reasons by elimination from the revealed roles and mutually supporting claims.
  *Do:* When the wolves are gone and you are one of the last 3-4 players, don't answer the room's elimination logic with a vague echo or an ambiguity argument. Name the one other unexplained player and build a concrete case on the gap in their story: their cleared status rests on a single unverifiable claim, or two claims vouch only for each other, and nothing is verified unless the Game Master announced it. Then push for a split vote on that player, since with 4 alive a 2-2 split means no elimination.
- key 56e0dfbd-6086-4171-835e-51e8866c4997 (score 0.779) *When:* Late in day 3 discussion, the sentinel, healer and investigator claims all point at me as the night-immune Serial Killer. My attempts to call those claims fabricated have been rebutted one after another. Information landscape: Information-rich and one-sided: a confirmed wolf death, a watch result matched by the healer, no save announced, an investigator clear, and no live evidence supporting my denials. Stakes: If the vote lands on me, my solo win condition ends and only the town and the wolves are left to contest the game. This discussion is probably my last chance to move even one vote, and a failed denial adds no information and only tightens the case. Consensus: Near-unanimous and evidence-based: claims cross-confirm, a wolf is confirmed dead, and my survival without a save announcement is read as night immunity. Alternative theories offered so far were rebutted on the spot. My position: Holding out alone against everyone. Heat: Maximal. Seven or eight of the nine living players have named me, the vote is imminent, and the case is built on interlocking claims and mechanical deduction rather than mood. Forward exposure: Each further denial of a true result, or a theory of coordinated fabrication, commits me to a story the record can test and cannot be retracted. A single redirect toward a living suspect costs little and can be dropped, but only if it fits the confirmed facts. Target landscape: The accusers' claims (investigator, sentinel, healer) interlock and each confirms the others. Their case rests on mechanics, such as surviving an attack with no save announced, plus vote records. Any other living player I could point at has little evidence against them, so a redirect has to rest on their votes or claim consistency. Public vs private: Privately I know the watch, the protect and the clear describe me accurately. Publicly the room has already deduced I am the Serial Killer, and my own denials only added contradictions.
  *Do:* When interlocking claims make your night immunity deducible and the room is converging on you, stop contesting each claim one by one. Spend your remaining speech on a single narrow case that doesn't contradict anything already confirmed, steering suspicion toward a living player whose claim or votes are weakest, and ask the village to weigh the wolves still alive before using the vote on you. If no living candidate has a checkable weakness, make no further claims that the record can contradict.

Stakes: Several eliminations remain before the serial killer can win, so the move is about staying unsuspected while the village thins out the wolves. A needless endorsement or accusation now costs more than staying quiet. Consensus: The village is fairly aligned on hunting wolves, based on a revealed wolf death, role claims, and vote records. No one is considering a third evil faction. My position: With the majority, echoing rather than driving. Heat: Low to none. You have spoken little, sided with the room, and no one has named you or raised the possibility of a lone killer. Forward exposure: Echoing the room's wolf-focused line costs nothing and can be reversed. Endorsing a role claim or naming a new threat commits you to a read that is hard to walk back and may expose a lone-killer hypothesis. You have not yet been asked for your role. Target landscape: The live candidates are wolf-side or suspect players already under discussion: a self-explained non-visiting neutral, a questioning wolf, and claimed investigative roles. The evidence against them is mostly claims and vote records, not hard proof, and you are not among them. Public vs private: Privately you know you are a lone killer that no one suspects, and the wolves are your rivals. Publicly the village believes the only remaining threats are wolves, so you sit inside the town-looking majority.
  *Do:* When the room is busy hunting wolves and no one suspects a lone killer, speak briefly in line with the majority and add sincere, behavior- or vote-record-based observations that keep the wolf-vs-town fight going. Avoid vouching for any role claim as true beyond saying it is mechanically consistent, so you do not help a confirmed town bloc form that later closes in on you. If someone asks for your role or reads, answer vaguely, in line with the room, instead of volunteering a new threat theory.
- key 56ca2564-d936-4a4c-b1c7-2b3b161b6769 (score 0.764) *When:* Day 3-5 with 3-4 players alive. You are asked to account for your role, and the town can test any claim against announced deaths, revealed roles, and the other claimants' statements. Information landscape: Fairly rich: many roles are revealed or dead, and the announced night reports constrain which role stories are credible. There is little room to bluff. Stakes: At 3 alive one more death gets you to the win condition, and one vote against you ends your game. A claim that fails the record check loses immediately. Consensus: The room has framed the vote as a choice between two or three players and is checking each claim against what has been announced. My position: Holding out against the forming consensus and under direct pressure to account for your role. Heat: High: you are named as one of the last suspects, and a late claim will draw an immediate challenge. Forward exposure: A role claim is checkable against announced events and other claims and is nearly impossible to retract. A role-less or already-disproved claim leaves you with no fallback story once challenged. Target landscape: The other survivors and their claims, with revealed roles (dead town and wolves) known to everyone. The open question is which unrevealed or unclaimed role you could plausibly hold. The basis is mostly timing and announced facts, not hard evidence. Public vs private: Privately you are the Serial Killer with no town ability to report. Publicly any claim is judged against the announced deaths, revealed roles and other players' claims, and a role already dead or already claimed by someone else will not hold.
  *Do:* If you must give a role story to escape elimination by exclusion, first check the public record and claim only a role that is not already revealed as dead and not already claimed by a living player. **Lessons to judge:**
- key 2e1b9a39-43b2-4d0b-84f8-00983c84b1bd (score 0.824) *When:* Night 2 after a night-1 death and an all-abstain day-2 vote. Stakes: Mid-game with several evil factions alive and no eliminations yet from votes. One follow can give the town its first concrete lead, but a misread visit could point at an innocent, so the result needs corroboration. Target landscape: Living players with the most public presence in the day-2 discussion, none with a confirmed role or hard evidence against them. Picks rest on behavior (who urged caution or steered the room), not on evidence. A follow only shows where the target went, not why.

Information landscape: Information-starved: your own private follow result, a few unverified claims, and a split vote record. No confirmed wolf identity. Stakes: Each night of lost trail information costs the town its only movement data while the wolves keep carrying kills. A wasted follow, or your death, removes your role from the board. Target landscape: Several living players: the previously followed or publicly accused suspect, plus quiet unclaimed players, a claimed Investigator or Sigilist, and neutrals. All have little or no trail data, and the suspect was chosen on behavior rather than evidence.
  *Do:* If you were roleblocked last night, or you have publicly named your intended follow target, pick a different living player to follow tonight, ideally one you have not mentioned. A repeat on a publicly known target is easy for the Chanteuse to predict, and in this cluster those repeats were blocked and yielded no result.

There are claims and behavioral reads such as one lone vote or one silent player, but no hard evidence tying anyone to wolves or the lone killer. Stakes: Mid-game with several wolves and a lone killer still alive. Wasting a follow on an already-plausible Town claimant costs a night of tracking, while a follow that catches a killer's visit can turn the next vote. Target landscape: Unvetted suspects (a silent player with a lone vote, an aggressive voter against you) versus claimed investigative roles (Investigator, Sentinel) whose claims are unverified; choice rests on behavior, with no confirmed evil flips.
  *Do:* On Night 2, follow the quiet or hostile player who has drawn a lone vote or suspicion but has not been checked, rather than a claimed Town investigative role.
