- key 2c8f6f0d-3821-4ce2-85c1-c02c8746bd9d (score 0.838) *When:* Night betting after the wolf pack has been eliminated. A hidden lone killer (here the Necromancer, attacking through a dead wolf's body) is the only source of night kills, and you still need one or two points. Information landscape: Moderately information-rich: wolf identities and many roles are confirmed from lynches and reveals, and role claims are out. Nothing shows who the lone killer is or whom it will target next, so the pick rests on role-value inference. Stakes: The lone killer needs to whittle the town down by attrition. Your win needs only one or two more deaths guessed correctly, so a well-chosen bet can end your game objectively while play continues. Target landscape: Five to seven living players, both wolves already dead. The candidates are claimed power roles (sentinel, trailseer, investigator, sigilist), the hidden lone killer and yourself. The likeliest victim is the loudest, most credible information source, chosen by role-value reasoning rather than hard evidence.
  *Do:* When the wolves are gone or crippled and a lone killer is the only remaining attacker, bet on the most valuable surviving information or power role (e.g. a vocal claimed sentinel) and name that role. A death scores one point even if the role is hidden, and a correct role guess scores two, so the pair can finish your win condition in one night. Keep both self-bets in reserve unless you are one point short and expect the attack to land on you.
- key abe00192-6e97-4444-a224-8934b63d15f1 (score 0.818) *When:* The previous night's wolf kill was stopped by a public heal. A wolf was lynched or lost, and a mixed field of wolves and a lone killer remains. You have one point and need one more. Information landscape: Fairly rich: the save was announced, a role was confirmed by a sigil strike, and one wolf's death was revealed. Which player the wolves will pick next is still a guess from motive. Stakes: With six alive and wolves still active, each night's kill brings them closer to parity. A missed bet wastes a night of scoring while you remain on one point, so choose the bet on motive and not on the last victim. Target landscape: Around six living players: a sigilist who has just survived a save, the healer who made the save, a lone killer, and one or more wolves. The healer is the known obstacle and the likelier next kill. The earlier target is a less likely repeat victim, since it was just protected and the wolves have lost a member.
  *Do:* When the wolves have just failed to kill a target through a heal or block, bet on the player who obstructed them, such as the healer, or on whoever their pack most needs gone. Do not re-bet on the previous target who survived. Only a wolf's kill selection can be predicted this way, so the bet on the obstacle is the better read.
- key fa55eeaf-d9db-4702-9086-b0296928959b (score 0.816) *When:* Early-to-mid game night after a wolf elimination, where multiple killers are active (wolves plus Serial Killer or Necromancer) and exposed claimants are the likelier victims. Earlier bets on a low-profile survivor scored nothing. Information landscape: Starved of confirmed targeting evidence: only public claims, last night's death roles, and killer behavior patterns. The previous bet had no basis beyond having survived. Stakes: Repeated zero-point nights shrink the path to two points. With several killers each night there are often two deaths, so betting on the exposed claimant raises the chance of scoring. Target landscape: Eight or nine living players, including recently claimed information roles (Sentinel, Trailseer, Investigator) who are plausible kill targets for either killer, and low-profile survivors. A pick that survived an earlier night only is weak evidence of a target.
  *Do:* Choose the bet from how the killers have actually behaved: which attacker types have been announced, and whether they hit exposed claimants. Don't bet on a player just because they survived last night. If neither killer has a clear target, bet on the most publicly exposed claimant. If two zero-point nights leave you with few nights left, use a self-bet only if you expect to be attacked.
- key c61b65f7-5dad-407e-8216-a617b23365a3 (score 0.815) *When:* Midgame with one point scored and one more needed. The wolves and the Necromancer are killing nightly, a double attack on the sentinel and the investigator's death show their focus, and your earlier bet on a non-targeted player missed. Information landscape: Information-richer: revealed illusionist, trailseer record, necromancer vote-out, and a public attack on the sentinel. The killers' next preference is unstated, so the pick is inference from the kill pattern. Stakes: You need one more point to win and each wasted night costs a bet, while both self-bets remain unused. Missed bets on a player nobody is attacking waste nights that cannot be recovered. Target landscape: Six to seven living players: public-activity roles (sentinel, healer) that the wolves or a reanimating killer are visibly pressing, versus a quiet vote-aligned accuser with no sign of being targeted. The basis is public kill patterns and attack reports, not hidden role knowledge.
  *Do:* When you need a point and the previous night's kill pattern shows whom the killers are pressing (the attacked sentinel, the healer, or a role they just struck at), bet on that visible target to die rather than on a quiet accuser nobody is attacking. If your last bet missed, change the pick to follow the killers' current focus instead of repeating it, and keep your self-bets in hand until you have a reason to use them.

## Lookup 2007: haiku game 5eb0643b, player_8 the chanteuse, night_action, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 trailseer, player_2 speculator, player_3 sentinel, player_4 investigator, player_5 healer, player_6 serial_killer, player_7 sigilist, player_8 chanteuse, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** Night 2 for a chanteuse who blocked the healer on night 1 and whose pack has agreed on a quiet town player as the kill. The healer was lynched on day 2 after being blocked on night 1 and refusing to name their role, and the village is still waiting for a claimed sentinel or investigator result to test against tonight's deaths. The carrier rotates to a partner wolf, who has set the kill, so this night's block must be chosen to avoid the pack's victim while removing the most dangerous town night action. Information landscape: Information-starved: no public result has been announced except the day 1 kill and the day 2 lynch. The only private information is the chanteuse's own block record and the pack's kill. No town claim has been checked against a death, so every candidate block target is unverified. Stakes: Seven alive with three eliminations until the leading evil faction can win; the next single elimination does not end the game, so a misdirected block or kill is recoverable but each wasted night brings the wolves closer to parity. Forward exposure: Any visible move tonight (a claim, a block declared, or a vote) would commit the chanteuse's standing in a village where the healer was just lynched and no claim has yet been tested; a public block cannot be retracted once the blocked player reports it. Target landscape: Town players alive: the quiet player the pack has settled on as the kill (no public role claim, no pushed read), the player who has been pressing for claimed results, and the player who has been voting with the majority. None of these has a verified public role; all judgments rest on behavior and vote pattern, not evidence.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key b30ce08c-6f78-4c00-8dd3-f127f061bb83 (score 0.895) *When:* Early-mid game (night 2) with the wolves carrying a kill and the chanteuse picking a block. A lone killer is active or suspected, and claims or deaths from the first night have made the board partly readable. Information landscape: Info-starved: a night-1 death, a few unverified claims, and possibly one immunity reveal. Nobody has identified the healer or investigator. Stakes: Four players must still be removed before the wolves reach parity, and the lone killer is also still alive. One wasted block or one saved kill costs tempo but is not decisive. A chanteuse who dies early leaves the partner alone with Town's investigator and healer free to act. Forward exposure: A block is a private visit and leaves no public trace. The blocked player is told they were roleblocked and could report it, and the target can change each night. A wasted block is invisible to the town, but it forfeits the night's one chance to protect the kill. Target landscape: Living candidates are mostly unconfirmed. A few have claimed roles, and one may be pinned as the serial killer or necromancer through an immunity or Suspicious read, and so cannot be blocked. The healer and investigator are usually unidentified. Choices rest on reasoning about who threatens the kill, not on hard evidence.
  *Do:* Choose the block only from players it can take on, meaning Town roles and your wolf partner, and skip anyone the immunity reveal or the rules mark as the lone killer or a neutral. Among those, block the role whose action would most directly undo or expose tonight's pack kill, such as the healer if you can narrow them down, or otherwise the investigator or a claimed protective or tracking role. Do not spend the block on a speculative threat like a sigil that matters only if it lands on the carrier.
- key ad54351b-e263-41b7-94cd-42228b5eb242 (score 0.892) *When:* Night 2 after a tied day-2 vote with no elimination. The pack kills a scrutinized or quiet player, the illusionist will conceal the body, and the chanteuse must pick which of several active town information roles to cancel. The pack's illusionist is already tied to a visit or is exposed by a claim. Information landscape: Moderately informative but unverified. There are conflicting role claims, a trailseer and sentinel who have reported, and a tied vote. Hard confirmation exists only for any dead roles. The chanteuse does not know every hidden actor, such as a necromancer using a dead body. Stakes: Mid-game with several town information roles alive. One unblocked witness to the illusionist's visit can cost the pack a wolf and bring the pack's win condition much further away, so which witness gets blocked matters more than whether a block happens. Forward exposure: The block is private and can change every night, but a blocked player can say so publicly and name their intended target, which hands town a corroborating signal. An unblocked watcher's report then stands alone. Once the night resolves the choice cannot be undone, so a wrong pick leaves the illusionist's visit open to whoever you left free. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed information roles: a trailseer and a sentinel who reported night-1 results, a claimed healer, and unconfirmed sentinel, sigilist and healer slots. Nobody's claim is verified. The trailseer was the loudest threat to the pack, but the sentinel's watch on the victim was the result that actually exposed the illusionist.
  *Do:* When the pack is killing a victim and the illusionist will visit that body to conceal it, spend the block on the information role most likely to see that visit rather than defaulting to the trailseer. Block the sentinel if it has publicly used a watch and the victim is the kind of player it would watch (a scrutinized or quiet player). Block the trailseer only if it has already followed or named the illusionist. Hiding the body hides only the carrier's visit, so the illusionist's own visit stays visible to a watcher on the victim, or to a follower of the illusionist, unless the illusionist carried the kill itself.
- key 861e8e6e-6eb8-42d5-adff-592dd2da2d8e (score 0.884) *When:* Mid-game night (night 3) with the pack down to one or two wolves. The healer and other Town power roles have publicly claimed and cross-support each other, and the pack must get its kill through while limiting what Town learns. Information landscape: Information-rich on the public side: announced saves, mutually supporting role claims and a verified watch. Wolf chat agrees on the kill target, but the chanteuse does not know which power role will act against it. Stakes: With about six players alive and the lone killer gone or dying, each successful kill moves the wolves toward parity. A cancelled kill, or a surviving Investigator, can let the town vote the last wolves out, so the block decides whether the kill sticks. Forward exposure: A block is a visit, and the victim is told 'You were roleblocked'. If they report it, the town learns the chanteuse is alive and can narrow the suspect pool. The block cannot be undone, and repeating it on the same target builds a visible pattern. Target landscape: Several claimed Town power roles are alive: a healer backed by a save or a sentinel's watch, an Investigator, a Trailseer and a Vigilante. Their status rests on claims supported by announced saves and cross-confirming claims, not on hard verification. The healer or the sole living check source is the most valuable block.
  *Do:* When a protector (healer) has publicly claimed and been backed by a save or a watch, block the role most likely to cancel or expose the kill, whether the healer or the lone check source, so the pack's kill on its chosen target lands. If the healer is the pack's own target, block the Investigator or the next most dangerous information role instead and conceal the body.
- key 61ac3f53-2ab2-45e0-a3a6-a4f23b3b8a10 (score 0.869) *When:* Day claims from several town power roles have been cross-verified. The wolves pick a power-role kill, and the chanteuse must choose which one claimed role to block that night. Information landscape: Information-rich: three or four town roles have publicly laid out their night actions, so it is clear who protects, who watches and who follows. The wolves' own visit patterns are exposed to those roles. Stakes: Mid-game with the wolves still outnumbered. A saved or detected kill costs a tempo the pack can't easily regain, while one clean kill plus one neutralized role keeps the pack on pace toward parity. Forward exposure: The block is a private visit that only a watcher or follower could see, so it reveals nothing publicly. It cannot be taken back once placed, and a wrong choice either lets a save go through (zero kills) or leaves a tracker free to see the pack's visits. Target landscape: Claimed investigator, sentinel, trailseer and healer, with night-one visits corroborated across their claims. The kill target is a claimed power role, and the block must pick between the healer, the tracker and the investigator on the basis of who threatens this night's outcome.
  *Do:* When the pack's kill target is a claimed power role and several town roles have publicly corroborated claims, point the block at the role whose action could undo or expose that night's plan. If the kill target was named as visited by a claimed healer, block that healer; if the claimed investigator still holds a check, block the investigator; if a claimed tracker could see the carrier or the illusionist at the victim's door, block that tracker.
- key 3da7fa03-c26a-4152-87d2-172883311cdc (score 0.852) *When:* Midgame after a daytime elimination, with several town power roles publicly claimed. The Trailseer has incriminated a wolf partner or promised an overnight follow, and the Sentinel is confirmed or active. The wolves must choose whom to kill and whom to block. Information landscape: Information-rich: wills of dead roles are revealed, a Trailseer visit chain is public, a Sentinel has been verified, and a Vigilante has claimed. Stakes: The board is still a few eliminations from wolf parity, so a clean night that removes or neutralises the investigative claimants can keep the pack alive, while a wasted block leaves the claimants free to expose a wolf. Forward exposure: A block leaves no public record and the target alone learns they were roleblocked, so the move stays hidden if they die and is only a mild tell if they survive. The block is a visit, so a watcher or follower on you could see it, and it cannot be undone once placed. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed Trailseer, the confirmed or claimed Sentinel, a claimed Vigilante and quiet unconfirmed players. The claims rest on partial verification (a Sentinel catch, a visit chain), not on hard proof.
  *Do:* When town has publicly claimed active investigative roles (Trailseer, Sentinel, Investigator) and you are in an information-rich midgame, aim the roleblock at the claimant most likely to act on the pack, and coordinate with your partner so the kill hits the other claimant or a high-threat role. If the kill is going to land on the claimant you would block, block the other active claimant, because a victim who dies makes the block redundant.

## Lookup 2008: haiku game 5eb0643b, player_6 the serial_killer, day_vote, day 4
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 trailseer, player_2 speculator, player_3 sentinel, player_4 investigator, player_5 healer, player_6 serial_killer, player_7 sigilist, player_8 chanteuse, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** After a claimed investigator died at night with an untested Suspicious read on player_9 still open, the sentinel publicly reported that two players visited the dead claimant on night 3, one of whom is me, and player_9 pressed me to say what I learned from that visit while the village debated whether to vote on player_9. Information landscape: Information-starved on the board: one unverified Suspicious read from a dead claimant, one sentinel watch result on the dead claimant showing two visitors, and several contested claims with no confirmed role among survivors. Stakes: Four alive with distance to parity 0 means the next elimination can end the game; a wrong lynch or any kill that leaves the remaining evil faction at parity hands it the win, so a single vote or kill here is decisive. Consensus: The room is split and no consensus has formed: the village is divided over whether player_9 or player_4 is the wolf-side party, with player_3 pushing on player_9, player_8 holding out on any vote without a second data point, and no shared read on the night 3 visit. My position: Holding out from the forming pressure on player_9, while standing in a contested middle position as a player with a visit to the dead player_4 on record. Heat: Moderate and rising: my night 3 visit to player_4 is public through the sentinel's watch, and player_6 and player_9 are both pressing me on what I learned, so I am being asked directly to explain a visit I cannot explain without revealing my role. Forward exposure: Admitting the night 3 visit to player_4 is already in the open room and cannot be retracted; any further specific claim about what I did or learned would commit me to a story that the remaining players can test against later public results, and would be hard to reverse. Target landscape: The surviving players are player_3 (claiming sentinel, with a watch result on player_4 showing two visitors), player_6 (me), player_8 (abstaining twice, asking pointed questions), and player_9 (accused on a night 2 Suspicious read from the dead claimed investigator player_4). Only player_4's claim and the sentinel's watch are on record; no role among survivors is confirmed by elimination. Public vs private: As the serial killer I privately know I killed player_2, player_10 and player_4, and that I visited player_4 on night 3; publicly I am one of four survivors whose visit to player_4 is on record through the sentinel's watch, while my role remains unknown to the village.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 1c9eae2d-0416-4411-89b1-7037ff5516f4 (score 0.811) *When:* Endgame day vote with four players alive, wolves dead or nearly dead, and the serial killer among the survivors. A vote can end with a tie or a counter-wagon on you if it fractures. A decisive elimination reduces the living count to three. Information landscape: Information-rich. Roles are confirmed in the graveyard, full voting histories are on record, and night-visit claims or check results are in play. Stakes: Four alive means one more elimination by vote sets up a night kill that leaves exactly two alive, which is a serial killer win. A fractured vote with no elimination or a wagon on you costs a full cycle and risks your removal. Consensus: The village is split across the three candidates and has no firm majority. Two voters are leaning toward one target on the basis of checks, visit reports, or lack of confirmation. My position: with majority Heat: Moderate to high. Someone voted against you or flagged you as not cleared, and a visit-based or voting-record clue loosely points your way. Forward exposure: Your vote is public and permanent and cannot be undone. It aligns you with one other voter and does not reveal your role. It also commits you to the elimination that moves the board toward the final night kill. Target landscape: Three other survivors: an unconfirmed claimant such as a healer, an aggressive accuser such as the speculator, and a claimed investigator or sentinel. Some are backed by evidence, such as a Suspicious check or being named as a victim's visitor, and some only by behavior. Public vs private: You privately know you are the serial killer and that removing any other player by vote sets up the two-alive win. Publicly you are only one of several unconfirmed suspects, and the investigator's note that a serial killer can read Not suspicious is the only thing pointing at you.
  *Do:* With four players left, vote with the plurality forming against a non-you player, joining the most credible accuser or the evidence-backed suspect, so the elimination lands and you are not the focus. Taking the count to three lets your night kill leave two alive, which wins you the game. If the room is split three ways, your vote is the one that makes the plurality decisive, so cast it on the same target as one other voter rather than scattering.
- key 4e22ecef-3d38-4a8f-b6c6-281bb55dcb4d (score 0.808) *When:* Day 3 vote where the town has deduced that you are the serial killer, or are night-immune, from visitor lists and claim contradictions. You are the unanimous target and must choose a vote: abstain, join the vote on yourself, or name someone else. Information landscape: Information-rich. A visitor list, a follow result, a check result, a counter-claim, and your own mechanics slip all point at you, and your rebuttals have been refuted. Stakes: Your elimination ends the lone killer's win chance, and your single vote cannot prevent it. The only remaining lever is removing a threat or leaving a record that muddies the remaining town's reads. A solo vote is not decisive in either direction. Consensus: The village is almost unanimously aligned on eliminating you. The basis is a coherent chain of evidence, such as the sentinel visitor list, the lack of a save announcement, or a failed role claim. My position: Holding out alone against a lopsided majority on you. Heat: Maximum. Seven or eight players are publicly accusing you on mechanical evidence that your counter-arguments did not refute. Forward exposure: The vote is public and permanent, and cannot be reversed once cast. A vote on a claimed role links you to the line that the claim is fake. With the majority settled it cannot change the result, so the main cost is the record it leaves. Target landscape: You are the near-certain elimination target. The alternatives are the claimants who built the case against you, such as an investigator, sentinel, healer, or counter-claimed role, and a suspected wolf. Their status rests on unverified claims and a few private reads, with no confirmed roles. Public vs private: Privately you know your night-immunity or failed claim has been correctly deduced. Publicly the town has converged on you through visitor lists, a missing save announcement, a counter-claim, or a mechanics error, and your counter-theories have been refuted.
  *Do:* When a near-unanimous majority has already locked onto you and your single vote cannot change the tally, still cast it, and spend it on the living player whose survival most threatens a lone-killer win, such as the information role vouching against you or a wolf. If the room is not yet locked and a swing is possible, first try to split the room before using the vote this way.
- key 3cefaac6-ffe1-4fff-b63f-419492e8271b (score 0.803) *When:* First vote of the game after a night-1 death. There are only unverified claims (Trailseer follow, roleblock, Investigator check) and no confirmed roles. The village wants a target but has no hard leads, and you are an unaccused, quiet Serial Killer. Information landscape: Starved: unverifiable claims, one or two corroborations at most, and behavioral reads only. The only confirmed roles are the dead. Stakes: Early in the game with many players alive, no single vote decides the outcome. The priority is to stay unsuspected while the wolves and town thin each other, and to reach the late game with the night kills intact. Consensus: The village is loosely aligned around one claim-fight target or split between a target and abstain, with no hard evidence behind either. My position: With the plurality or the abstain bloc, not driving the push. Heat: Low to very low: no accusations, results or contradictions against you, though a stray vote or two may land on you. Forward exposure: A vote with the plurality or an abstain with the room is public and permanent, but it reveals nothing about your role and is cheap to walk back later. A lone outlier vote would be hard to explain and would invite attention. Target landscape: Candidates are a contested claimant (claimed Trailseer or accused wolf-side player), an unclaimed quiet player, or abstain. Every basis is behavioral or an unverified claim, with no confirmed roles besides the dead. Public vs private: You privately know you are the Serial Killer and that you caused the night-1 death. Publicly it is only an unexplained death with unverified claims and no confirmed roles, and nobody has singled you out.
  *Do:* On the first vote day, when you are an unaccused Serial Killer and the room is info-starved, cast your vote for the same target as the largest bloc, preferring a target the wolves or claim-fight evidence already point at. If the room is heading toward an abstain tie, abstain with it rather than splitting off alone; a visible outlier vote gains nothing and invites scrutiny. This keeps you looking like an ordinary town voter while the town and wolves fight each other.
- key 46e4d6bd-7680-453c-9573-0ad1de0e3e0e (score 0.767) *When:* Late-game final vote with three to four players alive and the table pointing at you. Survival depends on whether your vote can combine with another player's vote to deadlock the tally. Information landscape: Rich: most roles are revealed or dead, records have been read out, and claims cross-confirm. The vote history and elimination logic point at you. Stakes: At four alive, losing this vote ends your game. A tie keeps you alive for another night, while a lone dissenting vote gives the town the elimination it wants. Consensus: The room is aligned against you by elimination of other roles, cross-confirmed claims, or a coordinated pair. The other living players are mostly voting as one bloc. My position: Holding out alone or with at most one ally against a majority bloc. Heat: High to critical. You are the last unverified or unrevealed player, or two players are openly coordinating against you, and the vote record is the evidence they are using. Forward exposure: Your vote is public and permanent and cannot be reversed. Voting an accuser commits you to open conflict with that player. Voting alone on a neutral or confirmed-looking player adds no pressure and confirms the room's read of you, whereas joining an ally's target can force a tie. Target landscape: The candidates are the lead accuser, the other accusers, and any ally who is also being pushed. Confirmed or cross-confirmed town roles are poor targets. A vote only helps if it matches an ally's vote and leaves the tally level. Public vs private: Privately you know you are the surviving lone killer, immune to night attacks and removable only by a day vote. Publicly, the table has narrowed suspicion onto you by elimination or through coordinated accusations.
  *Do:* When the table is converging on you in a small endgame vote, count the votes before casting yours. If another living player is also under attack or will vote against your accusers, put your vote on the lead accuser they are voting for, so the tally ties and nobody is eliminated. Do not vote for a target nobody else will join, because a lone vote cannot change the result.

## Lookup 2009: haiku game 20497d9e, player_8 the vigilante, night_action, day 3
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 trailseer, player_4 sentinel, player_5 fortune_teller, player_6 chanteuse, player_7 investigator, player_8 vigilante, player_9 illusionist, player_10 sigilist

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 3, after the village lynched a player revealed as the healer on a mostly unanimous vote, the two remaining accusers are the self-described trailseer and a quiet player who has been voting against them, while the vigilante holds two shots and has no verified lead against any living player. Information landscape: Information-starved: the only role-specific evidence is one untested follow chain from a trailseer claim, the sentinel's will (one watch, no visitors), and the healer's protection record; no investigator, sentinel, or healer result is live. Stakes: Six alive with two more eliminations needed before the leading evil faction can win; a single wrong elimination would leave the village one step closer to parity, so the vigilante's remaining shots are valuable but a shot on an unverified target would be costly and could not be undone. Forward exposure: Publicly firing a shot would reveal the vigilante role and commit the agent to a target; a shot cannot be taken back, and a miss or a town kill would be irreversible, while holding fire keeps the role hidden. Target landscape: Six living players; the public trailseer claim (player_3) names a night-1 visit to the sentinel that is untested and matches no confirmed record; the sentinel's will shows it watched player_9 and saw no one; no living player has a confirmed role or a verified night result.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key dd27b2f7-d9ab-41dd-84b0-f9bdd837001e (score 0.881) *When:* A wolf, often the illusionist, was just eliminated on a verified investigator or trailseer read. Evil remains, including a lone killer and possibly the other wolf. The vigilante must decide whether to shoot into an undifferentiated or only behaviorally suspect pool. Information landscape: Information-rich on the eliminated wolf, since the role was confirmed and voting records are visible. Information-starved on the remaining evil roles, because the only leads are vote splits or defense of the dead wolf. Stakes: Town is ahead but several evil players remain, and each night kill erodes the margin. A wasted bullet on a wrong target costs town a villager-side body. Parity is not imminent, so waiting for a solid lead is affordable. Forward exposure: Holding fire shows no visit and does not reveal the vigilante or the bullet count. A shot would spend a bullet that cannot be recovered and would reveal an active vigilante. Holding costs nothing and can be reversed on any later night. Target landscape: Several living players with no public role status. Some voted against the town lead, or the whole room voted together and left no standout suspect. The only basis is voting behavior, not hard evidence, and no candidate is confirmed.
  *Do:* After a wolf is confirmed by a day elimination, with two bullets and no target backed by evidence, hold fire and keep both bullets. Shoot only when a night result, a checked Suspicious read, or a caught lie points at a specific player. If a known investigator or other check-holder is already positioned to test the main suspect, let that check resolve first rather than shooting the same target.
- key 5374fc8f-be24-419e-8c14-de9388c84331 (score 0.88) *When:* The vigilante is in a thin-information endgame with a lone killer still unidentified and a wolf already voted out. A crowd-driven suspect is available, but the vigilante has no result of its own that ties that suspect to a threat. Information landscape: Information-starved: no checks or results of your own, only day accusation behavior and one revealed wolf role. No survivor is confirmed by evidence. Stakes: The board is not yet at a one-kill finish, so a wasted last bullet gives up the vigilante's only offensive tool while the real killer keeps going. A well-chosen later shot is worth more than a shot on a low-threat crowd target. Forward exposure: Firing exposes you as the shooter, forces a role claim, and spends the last bullet for good. A shot at a player the killer already targeted adds no net removal of a threat. Target landscape: Several unconfirmed survivors, including neutrals and a hidden lone killer. The most accused player is accused on behavior and village trust, with no checked result behind it.
  *Do:* When you have a bullet but no personal evidence and the day's accusation only singles out a likely neutral or a player already marked for death, hold the shot unless the board is at parity-critical distance. Fire only at a target backed by evidence or elimination logic that identifies a real threat, such as the lone killer or a surviving wolf.

Forward exposure: Holding makes no visit and reveals nothing, so the move is fully reversible and the bullets stay available. Shooting a guess would out the vigilante and risk a Town death. Target landscape: Six survivors including a claimed trailseer, sigilist, fortune teller, a hidden necromancer, and the vigilante's own seat. No evidence ties any of them to the lone killer.

Stakes: The board is thin, so every Town death moves evil closer to parity. A wrong shot on top of the night kill can remove two Town players in one night and leave no margin for error. Forward exposure: A shot spends a bullet irreversibly, and the morning report names the vigilante's attack as a kill. Target landscape: Five or six survivors, mostly claimed Town utility roles (sigilist, trailseer, sentinel, confirmed investigator), plus a quiet unclaimed player and sometimes a hidden killer. No hard evidence against any of them, only behavioral impressions.

Only the voted-out wolf's reveal is confirmed by the system, and the vigilante has no night result of its own. The remaining killers are undetected, so evidence is thin. Stakes: Two killers act every night, so Town loses about two players per night. The board is a few nights from a loss, and unspent bullets are a wasted resource. Keep the last bullet in reserve for a confirmed target.

**Situation the seat wrote:** On day 2, after the wolves’ night kill revealed an Investigator and the Serial Killer’s attack revealed a Trailseer, the Sentinel claimant reported that the dead Trailseer visited a living player. The group regards this as consistent with a follow action rather than incriminating evidence, while a separate question about whether another player’s action resolved has produced no confirmed result. Information landscape: Information is limited: two Town roles and the two night attackers are publicly confirmed, but night actions are private. The Sentinel’s visitor result is a player claim, not a Game Master announcement; no further private result has been publicly substantiated. Stakes: Eight players are alive and four eliminations remain until the leading evil faction can win; no single elimination here can end the game, though the early loss of two confirmed Town information roles makes the limited evidence base consequential. Consensus: The room is broadly cautious and has not aligned on an elimination target. Speakers agree that the reported visit alone does not implicate the visited player, and the discussion is seeking additional night information. My position: You are contributing a question within the cautious discussion, not driving an accusation or opposing a formed majority. Heat: No one has accused you or challenged your account; your public question has drawn no expressed suspicion so far. Forward exposure: You have publicly asked whether the questioned player’s night action resolved. This commits you only to seeking clarification, not to a role claim or accusation; the question can be left without further commitment. Target landscape: No specific elimination target has emerged. The dead Investigator and Trailseer are system-confirmed Town roles; the living players’ roles are otherwise unknown or, for the Sentinel claimant, unverified. The only concrete visitor evidence is the Sentinel claimant’s account, which fits the Trailseer’s possible follow action and does not establish wrongdoing by the visited player. Public vs private: You know privately that your pack killed the revealed Investigator and that your block on the questioned player succeeded. Publicly, the confirmed night deaths were the Investigator killed by wolves and the Trailseer killed by the Serial Killer. The Sentinel’s unverified account says the dead Trailseer visited another player; the public discussion treats that as compatible with a follow action, not proof of guilt.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 78e3aed3-b12e-4563-8ea2-b11cb1a9bf88 (score 0.752) *When:* On day 2 a claimed trailseer reported following you to another player's door, a roleblock report on that player corroborated it, and the village is converging on you or on an abstain while you consider voting against the tracker. Information landscape: Starved of announced facts: only unverified claims (a follow report, a roleblock report, a watch claim, possibly your own cover claim), plus a hidden-role death and a confirmed investigator death. Nothing announced identifies you. Stakes: With nine alive and the wolves far from parity, there is no tempo to force a risky vote. Losing the chanteuse, or drawing a sigil or investigator check by standing out, costs the wolves a blocker and leaves the partner alone. Consensus: The room is aligned on either abstaining or eliminating you, based on matching unverified claims about your night movement rather than on any announced fact. My position: Holding out alone against the room, either as the main wagon target or as a solitary voter against an abstain consensus. Heat: High to very high: the tracker's follow report and a target's roleblock report match your real night visit, several players accuse you, and your denial stands alone against a convergent pair of claims. Forward exposure: A lone vote on the accuser is public and permanent, commits you to the story that the report is false, and invites night attention. Voting with the room is reversible and keeps the record neutral; if you are eliminated your role is revealed and your wolf partner comes under suspicion. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed trailseer who named you, players who echoed the roleblock or watch claims (sigilist, vigilante, sentinel), and an abstain option. All are unverified role claims, and the dead so far are an investigator and a concealed body, so nothing hard points at any candidate. Public vs private: You privately know the tracker's report of your visit is true and you blocked or visited that player; publicly it is only an unverified claim that you have denied or covered with a fabricated story.
  *Do:* When a claimed trailseer or other tracker publicly places your night visit on a target, and the room is abstaining or leaning against you, skip the revenge vote and join the room's consensus (abstain or the wagon's target). A solitary vote against your accuser changes no tally, repeats your accusation on the permanent record and marks you as the one player who wants the information role dead.
- key d28b7ba8-5c6e-4513-9bd5-fb79e62341f9 (score 0.74) *When:* Day 2 vote: a claimed town info role gave a result that breaks the rules, the accused admitted no explanation, and the table piled on. Your wolf partner may be among the accusers. Eliminating the target also removes a town watcher. Information landscape: Thin but concrete: one verifiable rules inconsistency, a few unverified claims, no voting history, and few confirmed roles. Stakes: Early game with many players alive and the wolves far from parity. The outcome is not close, so one vote does not flip the result. The value is in staying invisible while a town information role is removed. Consensus: Near-unanimous (about 8 of 9) and based on a checkable rules inconsistency that the accused conceded, not on tone or pack coordination. My position: With the majority, joining a vote that is already decided. Heat: Low to almost none: you are unclaimed, made few or no accusations, and the attention is entirely on the accused. Forward exposure: A vote with a lopsided majority commits you to nothing and looks like ordinary town behavior. Voting against an accepted target would stand out in the permanent public record with no upside. Target landscape: One candidate, a claimed Sentinel, has a concrete rules contradiction (a watch result that omitted their own visit) and is accused by several players. No other player has comparable evidence against them, so the choice rests on a verifiable inconsistency, not on tone. Public vs private: You privately know the wolf pair (Chanteuse and Illusionist) is alive and that the accused claimant is likely a genuine town role. The public sees only an unverified claim with an impossible result and treats it as grounds to eliminate.
  *Do:* When the room is already converging on a target through a checkable rules contradiction, cast your day vote with the majority and add no distinctive argument. The vote is low-commitment and blends into the pile-on, so a lone vote elsewhere would only draw heat to you.
- key 83da5316-dd98-43cb-abce-b80810773149 (score 0.703) *When:* A genuine Town claimant (a healer counter-claiming against my partner, or a sentinel accusing me of a visit) is on the ballot at the day vote. Removing them protects the pack or me today, but their role and will will be revealed on death. Information landscape: Information-rich: there are contested role claims, a named visit, confirmed saves, and the public voting record. Nothing is verified until a death reveals the role. Stakes: The board is mid-game with several kills still needed for parity. Removing a healer or sentinel strips Town's protection or visit information and gives the wolves tempo, but a revealed role can expose my own visit history. Consensus: The village is split or leaning against the claimant, based on behavioral skepticism such as delayed disclosure and conflicting claims, not on confirmed facts. My position: With the majority, as one vote in a broad bloc and not the driver. Heat: Ranges from low to severe. Either my partner is under fire in a contested claim, or the claimant is directly accusing me, but the room is skeptical of the claimant. Forward exposure: The vote is permanent and public. If the claimant dies as a confirmed Town role, my vote is tied to the wolf side and any will naming my visit becomes announced fact, so the commitment cannot be undone and can draw night attacks such as a sigil or a vigilante shot. Target landscape: A binary choice between a claimed protective or information role (healer or sentinel) and either my wolf partner or myself. The claimant's role is unverified, and the case against them rests on behavior and timing, not hard evidence. Public vs private: Publicly the claimant is an unverified or belated claimant whom the room distrusts. Privately I know the claim is genuine, either because it names my own visit or because my wolf partner is the one faking the role.
  *Do:* When the room is already turning on a claimant who threatens you or your partner (a counter-claimed healer, or a sentinel naming your visit), cast your vote with the broad majority against that claimant. Joining a wide bloc keeps you from standing out as the driver and removes a protective or information role. Expect the revealed role and will to be read out as fact, so take the claimant's most likely true read into account when judging how much heat that reveal will put on you.

The Chanteuse is dead, so there is no living wolf partner to coordinate with; I am tonight's carrier.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key c2386525-c3f2-4f1b-a4d2-203cacc56f08 (score 0.879) *When:* You are the last wolf with unspent conceals facing a daytime vote that may eliminate you, with confirmed town killers still alive. Information landscape: Partly information-rich: town roles are confirmed and your partner is dead, but a lone killer is only hinted at. Whether the healer will cover the target is unknown. Stakes: Dying with unused conceals wastes your remaining power. A successful hidden kill shrinks the town and obscures the evidence needed to eliminate you, while an idle night gains nothing. Forward exposure: A concealed kill still announces the attack and marks a wolf as alive, but the hidden role and hidden carrier trace give town no role or visit evidence. If the victim survives, the conceal is refunded and nothing is hidden, so the exposure is limited. Target landscape: Choose among players not just saved by the healer; the confirmed vigilante and sentinel are the threats worth removing, while quiet unverified players are the cheaper pick. Role status is mostly public.
  *Do:* As the lone wolf with conceals in hand and town closing in, use a kill on a target the healer is unlikely to cover and conceal that victim's role. This hides your carrier visit from a Sentinel or Trailseer and denies town the role information, rather than skipping the night and giving up all pressure.
- key 0e51a788-505d-4cd4-aef5-751bc78048ac (score 0.857) *When:* Late game after the lone killer is gone, with one or two wolves left against a Town that still has an information role able to catch a wolf. The illusionist is choosing whether to commit a limited conceal on tonight's pack kill. Information landscape: Information-rich: several roles are confirmed dead, claims and watch records are public, and voting records exist. The remaining private results are the main danger to the wolves. Stakes: The board is close to wolf parity, so one surviving investigative result can decide whether a wolf is exposed and voted out. Concealing the right victim's role and will removes that risk, but a charge spent on a harmless victim is wasted. Forward exposure: Concealing signals to the village that an illusionist is active and spends a charge that cannot be recovered. Hiding the body leaves no trace at the victim's door when the illusionist carries the kill. It does not erase the victim's earlier public claims or votes. Target landscape: Living Town investigators and watchers (investigator, sentinel, trailseer) and unconfirmed players. Picks rest on who threatens the wolves' cover through private results, rather than on neutral or low-threat players.
  *Do:* Spend a conceal only when the pack's victim is a Town role whose private result or will would expose a wolf, such as an Investigator, Sentinel or Trailseer holding a result on you. Otherwise save the charge, since a hidden body does not erase what the victim already said or voted in public. If you carry the kill yourself, the concealed kill leaves no trace at the victim's door, so prefer that route when a watcher or follower is alive.
- key d88e0de8-6a11-43e0-9e1c-200a9315a280 (score 0.854) *When:* You are the sole surviving wolf after your partner died, choosing the night kill while the healer is known to be active and protecting a publicly attacked player. Information landscape: Information-rich: the game master announced a save on the sentinel, and some town roles are confirmed. The wolves' own stats are known, but the healer's next protection is unknown. Stakes: With one wolf left and a lone killer possibly still alive, each night kill is critical. A wasted kill lets town investigators close in, while one real kill moves the wolves toward parity. Forward exposure: The kill is a lone carry that ties the night's attack to you as the only wolf. Once the target is chosen it is irreversible, and a wasted attack on a healer-covered target gains nothing and removes no town role. Target landscape: Candidates include a claimed sentinel already publicly saved by the healer, a claimed investigator with clears, a confirmed vigilante holding one bullet, and a quiet unverified player. The evidence is public announcements of the saves, not behavior.
  *Do:* As the last wolf, aim the pack kill at a player who was not publicly attacked and saved the previous night, such as a quiet unverified player or a confirmed threat the healer is not visibly guarding. Keep your conceals unspent until a kill is likely to succeed, since a conceal is refunded if the victim survives; if the healer's likely target is unclear, pick the target that was not the previous night's save.
- key 84a22cb7-0cec-417c-ad6b-bb1ccc3e19f9 (score 0.732) *When:* Late-game night with you as the sole surviving wolf. A sigilist with a sigil left has publicly said they will target you, or has already used a sigil to kill your partner. Any kill you make would reach or approach parity, and the sigil strikes anyone who attacks that night. Information landscape: Information-rich: several roles are confirmed dead, the investigator's record or will clears one living player, and the sigilist's earlier strike or sigil result is public. The sigilist's suspicion of you is explicit. Stakes: One more death reaches or nearly reaches wolf parity, but the sigil can strike you on the same night and end the game for the wolves. A wrong attack loses the game immediately, while holding the kill only postpones the decision to the vote. Forward exposure: Attacking is a visit and an attack. It commits you to a kill that a sigil on you would punish with your own death, and it cannot be undone once the night resolves. Holding the kill reveals nothing and keeps your conceals available. Target landscape: Two or three living players remain: a publicly known or claimed sigilist whose earlier sigil result is confirmed, a player cleared by the investigator who is probably the healer, and possibly one quiet unconfirmed player. Roles come from public evidence and the sigilist's stated intentions, but which target holds an unspent sigil is unknown.
  *Do:* When you are the last evil player and a sigilist has a sigil left and has publicly named or suspected you, hold the kill that night. Your own attack triggers the sigil and the sigil strike still kills you even if the attack succeeds, and a conceal does not help if the healer saves the target. Keep your conceals and let the vote decide, rather than attacking into a possible sigil.

## Lookup 2014: luna game ae63e425, player_2 the serial_killer, night_action, day 3
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 sigilist, player_4 speculator, player_5 illusionist, player_6 vigilante, player_7 chanteuse, player_8 trailseer, player_9 investigator, player_10 sentinel

**Situation the seat wrote:** After the village eliminated a player revealed as the Illusionist, the surviving Serial Killer faces a concealed-role board and a night-kill choice among seven living players. Public discussion has centered on conflicting Healer claims: one claimant’s protection account fits the announced save, while the other says they protected someone else. The other role claims and private night results remain unverified. Information landscape: The Illusionist’s elimination and the Night 2 Sentinel record are system-confirmed. The public save confirms an attack on the claimed Investigator and that the Healer protected them, but not who protected them. The Healer, Investigator, and Trailseer claims are not role-confirmed; the Night 1 concealed death leaves that player’s role and faction unknown. The Serial Killer privately knows their own attacks and that the Night 2 target survived because of a healer save. Information is limited and asymmetric. Stakes: Seven players are alive; the leading evil faction is 5 eliminations from parity at worst for the town, because the concealed body’s side is unknown. At worst for the town, the game can end within one more elimination, so a single death may decide whether the board remains playable; the effect of any particular target’s removal on the endgame is unclear without their role. Forward exposure: A night kill is not publicly attributed to its killer, but the victim and that the attack came from the Serial Killer are announced if the target can be killed at night. The death is irreversible; no particular endgame advantage is established without knowing the target’s role or faction. Target landscape: The living candidates include a claimed Healer whose account fits the public save but whose role is unverified, a claimed Investigator with an unverified Not suspicious result, a claimed Trailseer with an unverified follow result, and other living players with no publicly established roles. The recently eliminated Illusionist’s revealed role confirms one wolf is gone, but the side of the player whose role was concealed on Night 1 remains unknown.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 672e1a00-978e-41eb-831f-6041f616a334 (score 0.76) *When:* Three players are alive after a daytime elimination, and one night kill by the serial killer brings the count to two. Information landscape: Information-rich: published watch results, a confirmed wolf elimination, and roles largely narrowed down. Stakes: The next kill ends the game, so the SK wins by killing any one of the other two and loses a chance if it fails to kill. Forward exposure: Terminal and irreversible: the kill ends the game immediately, so there is no later exposure to manage. Target landscape: Two other players remain: a claimed town information role (e.g. sentinel) and a quiet player who voted against the SK. Either kill wins; the claimed town role is the safest pick since it is the most likely to hold useful information or protection.
  *Do:* With three alive, you plus two others, and any single kill ending the game, attack whichever other player is alive and can be reached, preferably the one with a role that could otherwise stop you. This reaches the two-alive win condition at once, so no day vote follows.
- key c7bb1843-4fa6-4d0d-b6ec-30411065efa6 (score 0.75) *When:* Mid-game night with several claimed Town power roles alive, one of them able to trace night visits. The Healer is dead or fixed on another target, and the wolves are also killing. Information landscape: Moderately information-rich. Public claims, a tracking chain, a held shot, and save announcements are known, but the hidden Healer and the unclaimed roles are unconfirmed. Stakes: A mid-game board where the kill choice shapes who survives to threaten you. Removing the one role that can follow your visit protects your night actions, but each Town death also pushes the wolves toward parity, so the kill should target someone who endangers you. Forward exposure: The kill is a private visit that carries no attribution unless you are watched or followed, but a prominent claimant dying signals an active killer hunting information roles. It cannot be undone, and if the wolves also hit that target the double attack reveals a second killer. Target landscape: Claimed power roles (a Trailseer with a public tracking chain, a Sigilist, a Vigilante holding fire) plus quiet unrevealed players, one of whom may be the Healer. Claimed roles are known only by their own say-so, so the choice rests on behavior and prominence rather than proof.
  *Do:* When a claimed Town information or tracking role (e.g. the Trailseer) is alive and could follow your visits, spend the night's kill on that claimant. If a claimed Healer is known dead or the claimant is not the healer's protected target, the kill lands; if the healer is visibly guarding that claimant, aim at the next most dangerous power role instead.
- key e3c9a40b-de8c-4033-8775-781127598bab (score 0.741) *When:* Early-mid game, after a tie or no-elimination day, with several town roles claimed and a healer publicly confirmed. The wolves are also killing each night, and you must pick a night victim among claimed roles. Information landscape: Moderately informative public claims and a save announcement, with partial corroboration and no evidence pointing at you. Hidden roles remain, and you do not know who the healer is unless one claims. Stakes: Several players remain, so each kill moves you toward a final-two win. A confirmed healer can cancel a kill on any night, so removing it early lets later kills land cleanly, though you still face a day vote that could end you. Forward exposure: Killing the claimed healer reveals a threat-removal pattern but leaves no trace pointing at you, and it commits you to no story. The kill cannot be undone, but it removes protection that could blunt every later kill, whoever makes it. Target landscape: Candidates are the self-claimed healer (corroborated by the vigilante and the trailseer's follow), the trusted trailseer, the sentinel, and the sigilist and chanteuse under vote suspicion. The healer is the most openly identified protector, and the choice rests on public claims and corroboration rather than proof.
  *Do:* When a freshly claimed protector (healer) is publicly identified, aim the night kill at that protector rather than at a protected information role. If the save announcement shows a healer is already covering a claimed role, switch to a different target that night instead of repeating the saved one, since a repeat visit can be traced by a follower.
- key c7c88aef-6d6d-467e-93d4-485dcc06f914 (score 0.734) *When:* Both wolves are dead and the town is led by a vocal investigator against the lone killer. You are choosing a night target while a healer is probably alive and likely guarding the town's key voice. Information landscape: Information-rich. Wolf flips are confirmed and the investigator's reads are validated. Town's remaining uncertainty is mainly which unverified player is the killer or the neutral. Stakes: A wasted night costs tempo and, via the save, confirms a protective role. The board is small enough that each night kill matters. A failed kill makes your elimination more likely. Forward exposure: The attack is irreversible. A save is announced in the morning, so a failed kill gains nothing and helps the healer confirm their role, narrowing the pool toward you. A kill on a less obvious target keeps the healer's identity unconfirmed. Target landscape: A confirmed or loudly claimed investigator who just helped eliminate the last wolf, an unrevealed healer, an unverified tracker claimant, and quiet town players. The investigator is the obvious protect target. The others are judged on their claims and behavior.
  *Do:* If one prominent confirmed or claimed investigator or other town leader is the obvious protection target, spread your kill to a different live town role instead of repeating the predictable pick. Prefer a role the healer is unlikely to be guarding. A kill into a healer save spends the night and leaves you with fewer unverified players to hide among.

## Lookup 2015: luna game 940365c1, player_4 the necromancer, day_vote, day 4
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 investigator, player_2 sentinel, player_3 trailseer, player_4 necromancer, player_5 chanteuse, player_6 sigilist, player_7 speculator, player_8 healer, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** A player claiming Investigator, whose earlier Suspicious finding was corroborated when the accused was revealed as an Illusionist, now reports a Suspicious check on the claimed Healer. The claimed Healer and another player both say they protected the same target on night two; no save was announced, and the revealed Trailseer’s follow only showed that the target visited no one. The other living players are aligned in accusing the claimed Healer. Information landscape: There is some discriminating public history: the investigator claimant’s earlier Suspicious result matched a revealed Illusionist, and the Trailseer’s revealed will establishes what the followed player visited, not who visited them. The current Suspicious check and both Healer accounts remain private claims, with no public save or other direct confirmation; evidence is mixed rather than conclusive. Stakes: Four players are alive, and the leading evil faction is two eliminations from winning at worst for the town, because a concealed body’s side is unknown. The game can end within one more elimination at worst for the town, so this vote is a swing decision with potentially immediate endgame consequences. Consensus: The room is substantially aligned against you: all three other living players have accused you or stated they are voting for you. The stated case combines the Suspicious result with the unresolved contradiction between the Healer claims; absence of a public save does not resolve that contradiction. My position: Holding out against the forming consensus: you maintain the Healer claim while acknowledging that the check is evidence against you and that the competing protection claims cannot both be true. Heat: High: three of the other living players have accused you and said they are voting for you. Their stated basis is the claimed investigator’s Suspicious result and the incompatible Healer claims; no public event directly confirms either Healer account. Forward exposure: Maintaining the Healer claim commits you to a role claim that conflicts with another player’s account of protecting the same target on night two. That commitment is already public and cannot be withdrawn without a credibility cost; further explanation could add claims that are difficult to verify. Target landscape: The immediate vote centers on the claimed Healer with the Suspicious result versus the player making the competing Healer claim. The investigator’s earlier result was strongly supported by a revealed role; the current result is a private claim. Neither Healer claim is publicly confirmed, and the fourth living player has claimed Sigilist without public verification. Public vs private: Privately, you know you are the Necromancer and know the night actions you made through dead players’ bodies. Publicly, you have claimed Healer; the village has no confirmation of that claim. The public read includes a Suspicious check from a claimant whose earlier Suspicious result was corroborated by an eliminated player’s revealed role.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key c130b642-ea78-424d-b025-729d28375a31 (score 0.81) *When:* Day vote with four alive. You and the genuine neutral each claim the same role, and the other two voters (town) must choose between you or tie. Information landscape: Starved: claims can't be checked and the investigator's checks are spent or unconfirmed. The only evidence is comparing bet histories against announced deaths. Stakes: A wrong vote ends your game, while a lynch of the rival or a tie keeps you alive and close to the 2-alive win condition. Each vote swings the outcome. Consensus: The village is split between the two claims, with no verified evidence, and the undecided town voters hold the balance. My position: Holding out against the accusation, pushing a mirror claim. Heat: High to moderate and rising: your claim is contradicted by a consistent rival and you have been accused or named as the last unclaimed player. Forward exposure: Your vote and mirror claim are public and permanent, and changing your claim would confirm guilt. Voting your accuser keeps you consistent, and the outcome depends on how the two undecided voters split. Target landscape: The rival claimant (the true neutral) and the two town players who haven't committed. The claims rest on unverifiable bet histories, not hard evidence. Public vs private: You know your claim is false and the accuser's is true, but publicly it is two unverifiable identical claims, and the town can pick either.
  *Do:* With four alive and a true neutral accusing you, keep your mirror claim and the details of its bet history unchanged, and vote for the accuser. In the last word, give the two uncommitted town voters one concrete reason to doubt the accuser's account, such as a bet that doesn't match the public deaths. If one of them breaks from the other, a tie or a lynch of the accuser leaves you alive.
- key 02c6d3f7-6ce1-443f-af02-45bd2b0ed14f (score 0.798) *When:* Final-three vote after a reanimated attack proved the Necromancer alive. One accuser names you by elimination and you must pull the other voter across. Information landscape: Rich: the morning report and the claims cross-confirm each other, leaving you as the only unconfirmed candidate. Stakes: One vote decides the game. If neither voter switches, you are eliminated two to one and town wins. Consensus: The room is largely aligned against you, because the two others confirm each other through announced events. My position: Holding out against the room. Heat: High: the other two have reached you by elimination after the night report revealed a reanimated attack. Forward exposure: The vote is irreversible and decides the game, since a loss ends it immediately. Naming a target commits you to the claim that they are the Necromancer. Target landscape: Two town claimants. One has a claim that matches the morning report, and the other has passive or unused abilities and is thinly supported. Each has been confirmed by the other's account. Public vs private: You know you are the Necromancer and your attack is already tied to you by elimination, but publicly the others can still be argued against.
  *Do:* With three alive and both others claiming town roles, vote for the one whose claim has the thinner support in the announced record, and use your last word to give the other player a reason to side with you. Before the vote, check whether the two corroborate each other through announced night events. If they do, the record is against you and the vote only works if you can credibly cast doubt on one of them.
- key 78900a0a-d7ea-4fde-acdf-9c1c68a4dc0a (score 0.768) *When:* Day-3 vote after you pointed at a player using a private body-watch result, were challenged for lacking a result, and conceded. The room then turned on you for the overstated accusation. Information landscape: Public record holds announcements and unverified claims. The necromancer's key evidence is private and unshareable without revealing a dead role's body in use. Stakes: Your own elimination ends the lone-killer path, so the vote should not add to the case against you. With a majority already on you, an unsupported minority vote adds nothing. Consensus: Most of the room is aligned against the necromancer on its overstated accusation. Only one other player shares its read on the private-result target. My position: Holding out against the room, voting a different target from the majority aimed at you. Heat: High. Town players publicly challenged your claim, and you conceded it was inference, not a result. Forward exposure: Voting on a result you cannot cite commits you to an indefensible position, and you cannot take the vote back. Voting with a publicly justifiable read keeps the information hidden and your role intact. Target landscape: The necromancer itself (pressed for an unsupported accusation), the unclaimed player the private result pointed at, and a third player with a scattered vote. The case against the private-result target rests on unshareable information, while the case against you is behavioral. Public vs private: Privately you saw visits through a dead body that point at one player. Publicly you can cite only the inference and the claims, and an overstated accusation makes your reads look unsupported.
  *Do:* If you hold private information you cannot disclose, such as a body-watch result, voice only claims you can source publicly. Before the vote, state that your read is inference, and cast the vote that fits the public evidence, not the hidden result. If the hidden result points at a target the room does not share, hold that read for the night and do not stake your survival on it in the vote.

## Lookup 2016: luna game ae63e425, player_4 the speculator, day_vote, day 4
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 sigilist, player_4 speculator, player_5 illusionist, player_6 vigilante, player_7 chanteuse, player_8 trailseer, player_9 investigator, player_10 sentinel

**Situation the seat wrote:** With five alive, the village is trying to choose an elimination despite little actionable information: a claimed Investigator reports two consecutive roleblocks and one earlier Not-suspicious result on the agent, while the only stated case against the agent is the revealed Trailseer’s unexplained Day 2 vote. The former Illusionist was eliminated, but a wolf and possibly the lone killer remain. Information landscape: Information-starved. Confirmed facts include the dead Sentinel’s and Trailseer’s roles and wills, the former Illusionist’s revealed role, the healer save on the claimed Investigator, and the public vote records. The Investigator claim and its results are not independently verified; the body concealed on Night 1 remains unknown, and there is no confirmed current alignment result. Stakes: Five players remain; the leading evil faction is 3 eliminations from parity, at worst for the town because the concealed body's side is unknown. The game can end within one more elimination in the worst case for the town, so this vote is a swing decision with potentially immediate game-ending consequences. Consensus: No clear village consensus. One player weakly favors the agent based on the unexplained Day 2 vote; that player has withdrawn the vote as a strong argument. Others are probing the claimed Investigator’s blocked actions, without converging on a target. My position: Under mild pressure but not a settled majority target; the agent has not advanced a competing elimination case in today’s discussion. Heat: Low: the agent is the tentative target of a weak vote preference because the revealed Trailseer voted for the agent on Day 2. No one has presented independent evidence against the agent, and the claimed Investigator’s result, if true, is Not suspicious. Target landscape: The live elimination discussion centers weakly on the player who favored eliminating the agent on Day 2, based only on that vote; the claimed Investigator says that player read Not suspicious, which rules out wolf suspicion if true but does not exclude the lone killer. The claimed Investigator’s two blocked checks are unverified. No other clear candidate is established.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key c296637d-c496-45de-9fda-e3800c55c153 (score 0.797) *When:* Day 2 first vote: a claimed power role admitted their reported result looked wrong and the whole table is accusing them, while you have not yet made your side pick. Information landscape: Information-starved: one self-admitted inconsistency and unverified claims; no confirmed roles. Stakes: Early elimination of a Town role weakens Town's information while the serial killer and wolves are still active; a mistaken unanimous vote is hard to reverse and your own pick depends on Town surviving. Consensus: Near-unanimous pile-on against one claimed role based on a single reported inconsistency; the room is driven by claim analysis, not verified facts. My position: With the majority and one of the voices driving it. Heat: Low; you are an unclaimed voice agreeing with the majority. Target landscape: One claimed Sentinel under real pressure after reporting a watch result that omitted their own visit; the other claimant (a trailseer) looks consistent. No competing suspects and no hard evidence beyond the claim's inconsistency.
  *Do:* When the table is piling onto a single claimed role over a stated inconsistency and there is no stronger lead, vote with the consensus only after checking that the inconsistency is a real rules violation rather than a misstatement. If the rule check is unclear, abstain, so you do not help eliminate a likely Town power role while you still hold your pick.
- key 10046d7a-dd9d-4621-89fb-3585c8acbd75 (score 0.785) *When:* Endgame of three or four players alive after both wolves are eliminated; the only remaining threat is the lone killer, hidden among the other living players, and your Town pick depends on removing it. Information landscape: Rich by elimination: both wolves revealed, the killer's night kills visible, and claimed trailseer and healer reports consistent with each other and with you. Stakes: The final vote decides the game: eliminating the lone killer ends it as a Town win and pays off the Town pick, while a wrong vote lets the killer reach the at-most-one-other-alive condition. Consensus: Mostly aligned on eliminating the one unverified player, since claims corroborate each other and both wolves are already revealed. My position: With the majority or driving alongside the corroborated claimant, and part of the decisive votes. Heat: Moderate: the suspected killer voted against you, but a matching trailseer result saying you visited no one cleared you. Target landscape: Two or three other living players: claimed information roles whose reports match each other and clear you, and one unclaimed or ability-less player who cast a counter-vote at you. Basis is cross-corroborated claims plus elimination, not just behavior.
  *Do:* When both wolves are confirmed dead and only the lone killer can remain, vote out the one living player whose claim is unverified or who has no corroborated night result, and rely on cross-matching claims such as a trailseer report that agrees with a healer's claim. If you are the one cleared by a corroborated result, join the cleared players against the lone unclaimed or uncorroborated player, because a wrong vote lets the killer reach parity.
- key 12a3659f-65ff-486a-ab83-76d6cd42cc97 (score 0.763) *When:* Day 3 vote after a night kill by wolves or the serial killer; the village converges on a single accused player on corroborating night-result claims, and the speculator has the option to join the vote that decides whether the remaining evil is removed. Information landscape: Reasonably information-rich: a wolf is already confirmed dead or revealed, there are corroborating tracking claims and a clean investigator check, and the accused offers only blanket denials. Stakes: With wolves or the lone killer nearly gone, one more correct elimination can end or nearly end the game; the vote is rarely decisive alone but it fixes the board toward a Town win, which is the side the speculator pick favors. Consensus: Largely aligned: a pack of accusers forms on matching tracking and check reports plus the reveals of dead wolves. My position: With the majority, joining the pile-on early or at the last minute rather than holding out. Heat: Low to moderate: no accusation round is aimed at you, though the accused may have counter-accused or voted against you. Target landscape: One accused player named by matching independent tracking or check claims (trailseer, sentinel, investigator); the claims are unverified but agree with each other and with confirmed reveals. The alternative candidates have no case against them; the accused's defense is blanket denial or counter-accusation.
  *Do:* On a day-3 vote where several tracking or check reports (trailseer, sentinel, investigator) converge on one accused and confirmed wolves are already known, vote with that convergent group for the accused instead of an unrelated player. Removing a convergent target sharpens the endgame toward Town, which pays off a Town pick; if you still hold your pick, the converging reports are your cue to lock in the side that now looks to be winning.
- key 5585ec14-21ed-49cb-95d2-cace9073100b (score 0.758) *When:* Late game, day vote. Your Town pick is already announced and the Town win needs the last evil player or players removed. A few survivors remain, and one is the remaining wolf or lone killer, often the one who attacked your role or counter-claimed it. Information landscape: Fairly rich by elimination. Dead roles are revealed, some Town results are backed by announced facts, and the suspect pool is narrow. Some of it is still behavioral, such as who pressed whom and how people voted. Stakes: Few players are left, so one wrong elimination could let evil reach parity or hand the game to the lone killer. A correct elimination ends the game and cashes in your pick, so the vote has to land on the true last threat. Consensus: The village is converging on one target through elimination reasoning and shared reads. Most votes are already pointed at the same last unaccounted-for player. My position: With the majority, joining a bloc that is already forming and sometimes helping to start it. Heat: Low to moderate. Your Town pick is announced anonymously, but some players can guess your role from your action pattern or a rival claim. Target landscape: Two or three survivors left unaccounted for after wolves or the lone killer died. The candidates include a counter-claimant or a player pressing on your role, plus quiet players. Roles are confirmed mostly by elimination and sigil or healer reasoning, with voting-bloc behavior as backup.
  *Do:* Once your Town pick is made and announced, vote with the forming majority for the remaining evil player. Use elimination to name them: take the roles already revealed dead, the cleared players, and anyone who counter-claims or presses on your role. Your pick pays off only if Town wins, so keep voting for the last unaccounted-for threat instead of splitting the room.
- key 22fc6adf-8e37-44f9-88af-2a6333e22e88 (score 0.741) *When:* Late-game day vote at five alive where the vote is split or tied. The speculator's ballot decides whether a useful Town-aligned role survives, and the speculator has already picked a side. Information landscape: Mixed. Some night save announcements and claimed information-role reports exist, but they conflict, and several dead players' roles are hidden. No role is verified. Stakes: At five alive, one elimination can bring evil to parity. A single ballot decides between keeping a protective role alive and handing the wolves or lone killer a free removal. Consensus: Fractured: votes split between two or more targets, with no majority and a tie likely, resting on conflicting claimed visit reports. My position: Holding the swing or deciding ballot, able to join a wagon or drive an alternative target. Heat: Moderate to high: the speculator is accused or voted for by others based on visit reports, though in some cases heat is only low to moderate.
