## Lookup 2019: haiku game 5eb0643b, player_6 the serial_killer, day_vote, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 trailseer, player_2 speculator, player_3 sentinel, player_4 investigator, player_5 healer, player_6 serial_killer, player_7 sigilist, player_8 chanteuse, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 2 after a night in which a speculator was killed by the serial killer and a trailseer died to the wolves, with a blocked player_5 refusing to name their role while several players press for a vote on that basis. Information landscape: Information-starved: no verified night results, no revealed Town wills beyond role reveals, and only claims about blocks and unnamed roles. Stakes: Eight alive with four eliminations until the leading evil faction can win; a wrong vote is recoverable but costly, and no single vote or kill can end the game this day. Consensus: The room is unsettled and fragmented, with no shared read; discussion is centered on demanding that player_5 name a role, and no coalition has formed. My position: Holding out from any forming consensus, since none exists yet; I am engaging with checkable points to avoid being the passive default vote. Heat: Low to moderate: player_5 has drawn several pointed demands and a light low-confidence vote lean from player_10, but I have not been named by anyone with a result. Forward exposure: Any visible claim about my night or any push against another player commits me to a checkable account; a Sentinel or Trailseer naming me would turn the visit into a public test, and a denial would be hard to reverse. Target landscape: Eight alive, all unclaimed on the public record except player_5 who says a night role was blocked without naming it; the accusations so far rest on behavior and unverified blocked-role claims rather than any result. Public vs private: As the serial killer, I privately know I killed player_2 on night 1, while the public record shows only that a speculator was stabbed by the serial killer; the village now knows the killer is alive and unnamed, and I have publicly said nothing about my own night, which is the gap I must keep hidden.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 3cefaac6-ffe1-4fff-b63f-419492e8271b (score 0.803) *When:* First vote of the game after a night-1 death. There are only unverified claims (Trailseer follow, roleblock, Investigator check) and no confirmed roles. The village wants a target but has no hard leads, and you are an unaccused, quiet Serial Killer. Information landscape: Starved: unverifiable claims, one or two corroborations at most, and behavioral reads only. The only confirmed roles are the dead. Stakes: Early in the game with many players alive, no single vote decides the outcome. The priority is to stay unsuspected while the wolves and town thin each other, and to reach the late game with the night kills intact. Consensus: The village is loosely aligned around one claim-fight target or split between a target and abstain, with no hard evidence behind either. My position: With the plurality or the abstain bloc, not driving the push. Heat: Low to very low: no accusations, results or contradictions against you, though a stray vote or two may land on you. Forward exposure: A vote with the plurality or an abstain with the room is public and permanent, but it reveals nothing about your role and is cheap to walk back later. A lone outlier vote would be hard to explain and would invite attention. Target landscape: Candidates are a contested claimant (claimed Trailseer or accused wolf-side player), an unclaimed quiet player, or abstain. Every basis is behavioral or an unverified claim, with no confirmed roles besides the dead. Public vs private: You privately know you are the Serial Killer and that you caused the night-1 death. Publicly it is only an unexplained death with unverified claims and no confirmed roles, and nobody has singled you out.
  *Do:* On the first vote day, when you are an unaccused Serial Killer and the room is info-starved, cast your vote for the same target as the largest bloc, preferring a target the wolves or claim-fight evidence already point at. If the room is heading toward an abstain tie, abstain with it rather than splitting off alone; a visible outlier vote gains nothing and invites scrutiny. This keeps you looking like an ordinary town voter while the town and wolves fight each other.
- key fe7ffec1-f7d1-4b77-90c5-c021efb20d24 (score 0.802) *When:* The first day-2 vote after night-1 deaths. The village is either leadless and abstaining or piling onto a contested claim. You are the unsuspected Serial Killer who needs to survive to keep killing nightly. Information landscape: Information-starved to moderate: a few revealed night-1 dead roles, some unverified claims, and no caught lies or voting history that point to you. Stakes: The board is early, with many players alive and far from the Serial Killer's win threshold. The vote itself is not decisive, so the priority is to stay off the suspect list and alive for the next night kill. Consensus: The village is aligned, either on a unanimous abstain in a leadless day or on a near-unanimous pile-on against a contested claimant. The basis is the room's own discussion, not anything you supplied. My position: With the majority, riding a consensus built without you. Heat: Low to near zero: no one has accused or mentioned you, and you have spoken little or only in support of others. Forward exposure: A vote matching the room commits you to nothing about your role and is indistinguishable from the other votes. Votes are public and permanent, but an abstain can be changed the next day, and joining a shared target is tied to the room, not to you. Target landscape: Around nine or ten living players, with no or only unverified claims. Candidates are a contested claimant or no one, and the basis is behavioral or mechanical speculation rather than hard evidence. You are not among the candidates.   *Do:* On the first day-2 vote, when you are unsuspected and the room has no lead or has already converged on a target, cast the same vote as the majority (abstain if the room is abstaining, or join the existing pile-on). Don't drive a new accusation or take a lone stance, because a vote identical to the room's carries no tell about your role and keeps your cover for night kills.

Target landscape: Three other survivors: an unconfirmed claimant such as a healer, an aggressive accuser such as the speculator, and a claimed investigator or sentinel. Some are backed by evidence, such as a Suspicious check or being named as a victim's visitor, and some only by behavior. Publicly you are only one of several unconfirmed suspects, and the investigator's note that a serial killer can read Not suspicious is the only thing pointing at you.
  *Do:* With four players left, vote with the plurality forming against a non-you player, joining the most credible accuser or the evidence-backed suspect, so the elimination lands and you are not the focus. Taking the count to three lets your night kill leave two alive, which wins you the game. If the room is split three ways, your vote is the one that makes the plurality decisive, so cast it on the same target as one other voter rather than scattering.

Stakes: Your elimination ends the lone killer's win chance, and your single vote cannot prevent it. Consensus: The village is almost unanimously aligned on eliminating you. The basis is a coherent chain of evidence, such as the sentinel visitor list, the lack of a save announcement, or a failed role claim. Forward exposure: The vote is public and permanent, and cannot be reversed once cast. A vote on a claimed role links you to the line that the claim is fake. The alternatives are the claimants who built the case against you, such as an investigator, sentinel, healer, or counter-claimed role, and a suspected wolf. Their status rests on unverified claims and a few private reads, with no confirmed roles. Public vs private: Privately you know your night-immunity or failed claim has been correctly deduced. Publicly the town has converged on you through visitor lists, a missing save announcement, a counter-claim, or a mechanics error, and your counter-theories have been refuted.
  *Do:* When a near-unanimous majority has already locked onto you and your single vote cannot change the tally, still cast it, and spend it on the living player whose survival most threatens a lone-killer win, such as the information role vouching against you or a wolf. The fourth survivor's position is not stated, so unanimity is not established. My position: You are the accused and are defending yourself against the forming vote; you are not driving the consensus. **Lessons to judge:**
- key a5910d45-8dc4-4221-b423-a182e25ae597 (score 0.854) *When:* Late game with three or four alive after a public reanimated attack. The Necromancer pool has been narrowed to a few players, I am one of them, and I must either claim a cover role or accuse the other suspect before the vote. Information landscape: Fairly information-rich: announced night attack types, revealed roles, public vote records and a narrowed suspect pool. The remaining evidence is unverified claims. Stakes: With three alive, one vote or one night kill decides the game. If the vote lands on me, Town wins at once; if it lands elsewhere, I am a night kill from winning. Consensus: The room is converging against me. The other suspects' accounts fit the announced night events, and a ballot of two or three players can tie or flip on a single vote. My position: Holding out against a forming consensus and trying to turn the swing voter. Heat: High. I was named the Necromancer by elimination or by a rival claimant, and a public reanimated attack points at whoever has been channeling it. Forward exposure: A fabricated role claim or a flat counter-accusation commits me to a story that the public night record or the real role holder can test. It cannot be withdrawn without confessing, and with three alive the next vote is final. Target landscape: Two or three living suspects, including me. Their roles are claimed but unverified, and the basis is claims plus the public reanimated-attack report, which fits whoever is suspected. Public vs private: I know I am the Necromancer and that my reanimated attack is already public. Publicly I am one of two or three suspects, and my accusers' claims fit the announced night events.
  *Do:* When the Necromancer pool has shrunk to about three and you are named, anchor your defense only in announced facts such as morning reports, public votes and revealed roles. Pick a cover story that no living player can contradict from a private record. If a living player's own record already fits the facts better than your story, shift to showing that their account explains the announced night events no better than yours does. Do not invent a detailed record, such as bets, that a real holder can contradict.
- key e86ef8e3-c9f4-4dbd-a82d-b4a7d945af2c (score 0.837) *When:* A claimed Investigator publicly accuses or reads you as Suspicious, around a night where a hidden-role death or a reanimated kill hints at a Necromancer. Stakes: Being voted out ends the necromancer's only win path, which is to be last standing. This day's vote is therefore existential, and heat must come down before it. Consensus: The village is converging on the claimant's read, and its only open question is how to verify it. The basis is the claim plus any partial confirmation, not hard evidence. My position: Holding out alone against the consensus. Heat: High and rising. Publicly the village sees an unverified claimant versus an accused player and is searching for a way to test it.

Offer a cooperative test, such as asking the claimant for the full check history and asking any Trailseer or Sentinel to speak up, and press the village to put its vote on a rival suspect if the evidence stays thin. A flat denial or repeated pushback only reads as fear of proof and leaves you alone against the room.
- key dd94987c-df28-4bc1-9404-3c8bd57f26e3 (score 0.837) *When:* Late game with about five players alive. A body-assisted attack has been announced and the town is hunting the lone killer among the few unconfirmed players, while you are one of them. The announced reanimated attack proves a necromancer is alive, so scrutiny falls on every unconfirmed player, and your unexplained votes draw questions. Forward exposure: Claiming to be outside the suspect pool commits you to a facade that cross-examination of your votes can break, and you cannot take it back once challenged. Staying inside the pool and giving consistent reasons commits you to little and can be adjusted later. Target landscape: A few unconfirmed players (you, a surviving wolf or neutral, and others) next to cleared or confirmed players such as a healer cleared by an investigator's will.   *Do:* When a reanimated attack has been announced and you sit in a small unconfirmed pool, put yourself in that pool openly and answer questions about your voting record with reasons that match the public record. - key 549afc3b-ce66-4252-be08-fd8a0983b073 (score 0.835) *When:* Night 2 after an early town-role death and an abstain vote.   *Do:* Spend the check on a living player that no claim, watch or follow has yet explained and who shows a behavioral oddity, such as a quiet or low-profile player, a lone accuser, or someone who slipped in describing their own action. Forward exposure: A vote with the plurality or an abstain with the room is public and permanent, but it reveals nothing about your role and is cheap to walk back later. Publicly it is only an unexplained death with unverified claims and no confirmed roles, and nobody has singled you out.
  *Do:* On the first vote day, when you are an unaccused Serial Killer and the room is info-starved, cast your vote for the same target as the largest bloc, preferring a target the wolves or claim-fight evidence already point at. ## Lookup 3013: luna game 940365c1, player_2 the sentinel, night_action, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 investigator, player_2 sentinel, player_3 trailseer, player_4 necromancer, player_5 chanteuse, player_6 sigilist, player_7 speculator, player_8 healer, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** After the village eliminated a player revealed as the Illusionist, the Sentinel faces a night watch with one use remaining. A player claiming Investigator has gained strong public support from the confirmed result, making them a potentially informative door to watch, while the concealed first-night death leaves the town uncertain about the full threat landscape. Information landscape: Information is mixed: the eliminated player's Illusionist role is system-confirmed, strongly supporting the Investigator claimant's private Suspicious result. The Sentinel's own prior watch found no visitors to the player watched, which gives no alignment information. The first-night victim's role remains concealed, and no public night-action traces identify the surviving threats. Stakes: Eight players are alive and, at worst for the town, six eliminations remain until the leading evil faction can reach parity; nevertheless, the game can end within one more elimination at worst for the town, so a single night outcome can be decisive. Target landscape: The remaining candidates include the player claiming Investigator, whose Night 1 Suspicious result was supported by the eliminated player's revealed Illusionist role, and other living players without publicly confirmed roles. The concealed first-night victim's role and the identities of the remaining threats are unknown; the watch could be placed on a likely attack target or a player whose visit pattern would clarify behavior.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key e6ee1b11-9e40-4bfe-8182-217534f0b554 (score 0.829) *When:* Night 2 after a day-2 vote or abstain, usually with the chanteuse revealed or dead. The sentinel is a known information role, may be blocked or killed, and has one watch left. Only one of the observed cases (the one where the watch was aimed at a claim-linked suspect) turned the watch into usable town information. Information landscape: Moderately informed. There are a few claims (tracker, block, investigator or vigilante) and one's own night-1 watch record, but the remaining wolf, the lone killer and the neutral are unconfirmed. Evidence is thin and mostly behavioral. Stakes: Town still has slack but is losing information roles each night. A watch that confirms a claimed shooter's visit lets Town trust that player's remaining bullets. A watch that is blocked, concealed or aimed at nothing leaves the sentinel with no further visit data. Target landscape: About eight living players. One or two have a public record (e.g. caught visiting a door on night 1, or carrying a contested claim) and may be a claimed actor's next target. The rest are quiet players with only behavioral reads. The likely kill victim is a poor watch target if the illusionist can still conceal the body.
  *Do:* On night 2 with your last watch, put it on a door where the result can corroborate or refute a specific public claim. Good doors are a player a claimed shooter or visitor is expected to target, or a suspect who already has a public record. If no door is testable, a blind watch on a quiet player gains little, so keep the watch rather than spend it on pure behavior.
- key e67326c6-d2fc-4d90-96cc-f05e3b1b647f (score 0.809) *When:* Day 2 ended without an elimination after rival investigator claims. A night-1 death with a revealed role, with a necromancer able to use the body, leaves the sentinel deciding whether to spend a watch on the most contested suspect. Information landscape: Moderately information-rich: a revealed night-1 death, a trailseer follow placing a suspect at the victim's door, conflicting claims, and no confirmed wolf. Stakes: Mid-game with enough town alive to absorb a miss, but a lone killer is likely active. A watch on the focal suspect can expose a body-attack that is otherwise invisible. Target landscape: A contested suspect placed at the night-1 victim's door by a trailseer follow, who clashes with a rival investigator claimant; alternatives are the other claimant, a quiet player, or the likely next victim. No role is confirmed, so the basis is a tracked visit plus behavior.
  *Do:* When a contested suspect exists (a tracked visit at a victim's door plus a rival claim) and you still hold watches, spend a watch on that suspect rather than holding it, and keep your choice of door private. The visitor list can reveal who is acting on them, including a dead player's name at the door that exposes a necromancer working a body.
- key daaa1ff8-e232-4479-ab38-0490e7e7db89 (score 0.787) *When:* After a key information role was voted out, the town cannot identify the necromancer or wolves. The sentinel is the last watcher, was just double-attacked and saved only by the healer, and is an obvious chanteuse block target. Information landscape: Information-starved: only a reanimated-attack signature and a revealed record, with no confirmed suspect. Reads on who pushed votes are behavioral and weak. Stakes: Moderate pressure: the sentinel has been a direct target, and a blocked watch loses both information and the use. Spending a limited watch on weak behavior reads risks yielding nothing at an important point. Target landscape: One unconfirmed killer suspect chosen on voting behavior with weak evidence, versus likely victims such as other power roles or oneself, whose door a wolf or killer must visit to attack.
  *Do:* If you have a watch left and the town has no hard lead on the remaining killer or wolves, first consider who is likely to be attacked rather than a behavior-only suspect. If you do watch a suspect, expect the chanteuse to block you, since a blocked watch is wasted and shows nothing, so prefer the watch on the likely victim.
- key 040f01ab-18f0-43e5-a7bc-c1fbcb658342 (score 0.78) *When:* Night 2 after an early death or two and a vote that was tied, abstained, or produced no lead. The sentinel has one watch left and must pick whose door to stand at. Information landscape: Information-starved: few revealed roles, no verified wolf identity, and only behavioral pressure points such as vote totals or who drives the discussion. Stakes: Mid-game with the wolves still at full strength. A visitor name from the last watch may be the only hard lead, and spending it well can unlock a wolf elimination the next day. The cost is losing your watches and becoming a visible kill target. Target landscape: Roughly 7-8 living players with no confirmed roles beyond the early dead. Candidates are the loudest discussion-driver or a quiet player who drew votes. They are chosen on behavior as likely next wolf kill targets, not on evidence.
  *Do:* On night 2 with your last watch and no hard leads, watch the player the wolves are most likely to attack next, such as the most vocal question-driver or a quiet player drawing votes, rather than holding the watch or watching yourself. If a visitor appears, you have a concrete lead on who went to that door. If none appears, it may point to a night-immune target or a different kill choice.

## Lookup 3015: haiku game 5eb0643b, player_7 the sigilist, night_action, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 trailseer, player_2 speculator, player_3 sentinel, player_4 investigator, player_5 healer, player_6 serial_killer, player_7 sigilist, player_8 chanteuse, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** As the sigilist with two sigils, you face a night where the pack or the serial killer will likely attack one of the six remaining players, and a sigil placed on whoever attacks will strike that attacker. The village lynched a healer who claimed a blocked role without naming it, so no one is publicly verified as a night actor; the question is whether a sigil spent on a behaviorally suspicious player would catch a wolf or a serial killer attacking. Information landscape: Information-starved: no investigator or sentinel result has been announced, the only verified fact is the night-one block on the lynched healer, and day-two accusations rest on behavior and claims rather than results. Stakes: Seven alive with three eliminations until the leading evil faction can win, so a single wrong sigil or lynch is recoverable but each sigil spent without a strike removes one of only two remaining traps, and a wrong target would leave the wolves or serial killer free to act at no cost. Forward exposure: Placing a sigil on a player is a visible visit-commitment that spends one of two sigils permanently; it only reveals itself as a trap if the target attacks, and a sigil on a non-attacker returns only 'no effect', which would narrow suspicion without spending the other sigil's worth of information. Target landscape: Seven alive: the surviving players are player_3, player_4, player_6, player_8, player_9, player_10 and you. No one has a public role claim tied to tonight's kill. Public role status: player_1 (trailseer) and player_2 (speculator) dead by night, player_5 (healer) lynched and revealed. The remaining players' roles are unknown; the only evidence is behavioral: player_10 pressed the lynch on player_5, player_8 and player_9 abstained, and player_5 claimed a blocked role without naming it.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 76f5f000-6264-4a96-9412-e691d6076356 (score 0.877) *When:* One or both wolves may already be dead. A killer keeps targeting a known town player, and the sigilist is tempted to put the sigil on that expected victim to counter the next attack. Information landscape: Role information is public, such as confirmed investigator results and verified healer saves. There is still no trace pointing at the remaining killer. Stakes: The remaining killer can keep striking, but the town is not on the brink. Preserving limited sigils for an identified attacker matters more than acting every night. Forward exposure: Holding commits you to nothing and reveals nothing. You keep both charges and can place one on any later night. Target landscape: The remaining candidates are likely victims, such as a claimed investigator who survived an attack or was healer-protected, or unvetted players with no attack evidence. None has been tied to a kill.
  *Do:* If no living suspect has evidence of attacking and the only candidates are likely victims or confirmed-Town players such as a protected investigator, keep your sigils that night. Wait for a suspect with an attack pattern. A sigil on a player who does not attack does nothing, and a likely victim is the one player who will not attack.
- key 89606edf-43f9-4c73-bfa3-5cc1f21c0e00 (score 0.875) *When:* Late-game night with one wolf or lone killer still active among five or six living players, and the sigilist is a likely kill target. Information landscape: Information-rich on dead evil roles, cleared players and voting records, but the last attacker is identified only by behavior and elimination. Stakes: Each night costs a player and the board is close to parity, so a sigil that strikes the last killer can win the game outright, and one held until death is wasted. Forward exposure: Placing a sigil is a secret visit that spends the charge whether or not it triggers, so it cannot be undone. A no-effect result means the target did not attack, which fits a non-killer or an attacker who held back, and it does not clear them. Target landscape: A few living unverified candidates, with the others cleared by the investigator or sentinel. The best suspect is picked from behavior, such as an isolated vote against a confirmed role or a quiet profile, because no hard proof exists.
  *Do:* When few players remain (about 5-6) and one killer is left, place a sigil each night on the most probable unverified killer, chosen from voting record and investigator or sentinel clearances, rather than holding both sigils. If that player attacks, the sigil strikes them, so you convert a read into a kill. With one sigil left, spend it on your best read, since an unspent sigil does nothing if you are killed first.

## Lookup 3023: haiku game 5eb0643b, player_8 the chanteuse, day_vote, day 2
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 trailseer, player_2 speculator, player_3 sentinel, player_4 investigator, player_5 healer, player_6 serial_killer, player_7 sigilist, player_8 chanteuse, player_9 illusionist, player_10 vigilante

**Situation the seat wrote:** Day 2 after a night-1 kill of a trailseer: a player publicly says they had an active night role that was blocked but refuses to name it, and several players demand a name before any vote, while no one has a checkable result yet. Information landscape: Information-starved: no investigator or sentinel results have been claimed, the trailseer who could have cross-checked follows is dead, and the only public evidence is the blocked claim itself. Stakes: Eight alive with four eliminations needed before the leading evil faction can win, so a wrong vote today is recoverable but would still shift the balance toward a wolf-led endgame; no single elimination ends the game. Consensus: The village is not aligned; the room is split between pressing the blocked claimant to name their role and treating the lack of any result as insufficient to vote on. My position: Holding out from any accusation, with the village focused on player_5's unverified blocked-role claim. Heat: Low to moderate: player_10 has said they would vote player_5 at low confidence, and player_9 and player_6 are pressing player_5 to name a role, so the pressure is on player_5 rather than on me. Forward exposure: Any claim of a night result or role now would be unverifiable and would commit me to a story that the block on player_5 and the night-1 kill can be tested against; retracting it later is not possible, so staying quiet keeps the option open. Target landscape: Eight alive; player_5 is the only player publicly claiming a blocked night role, with no name and no result, so the claim rests on the blocked state alone; other players have made no claims, so no one has public role evidence against them. Public vs private: Privately, as the Chanteuse, I know my pack killed a trailseer on night 1 and that I blocked player_5 that night; publicly I have claimed nothing and hold no night result, and player_5 is publicly claiming a blocked active role without naming it.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 78e3aed3-b12e-4563-8ea2-b11cb1a9bf88 (score 0.813) *When:* On day 2 a claimed trailseer reported following you to another player's door, a roleblock report on that player corroborated it, and the village is converging on you or on an abstain while you consider voting against the tracker. Information landscape: Starved of announced facts: only unverified claims (a follow report, a roleblock report, a watch claim, possibly your own cover claim), plus a hidden-role death and a confirmed investigator death. Nothing announced identifies you. Stakes: With nine alive and the wolves far from parity, there is no tempo to force a risky vote. Losing the chanteuse, or drawing a sigil or investigator check by standing out, costs the wolves a blocker and leaves the partner alone. Consensus: The room is aligned on either abstaining or eliminating you, based on matching unverified claims about your night movement rather than on any announced fact. My position: Holding out alone against the room, either as the main wagon target or as a solitary voter against an abstain consensus. Heat: High to very high: the tracker's follow report and a target's roleblock report match your real night visit, several players accuse you, and your denial stands alone against a convergent pair of claims. Forward exposure: A lone vote on the accuser is public and permanent, commits you to the story that the report is false, and invites night attention. Voting with the room is reversible and keeps the record neutral; if you are eliminated your role is revealed and your wolf partner comes under suspicion. Target landscape: Candidates are the claimed trailseer who named you, players who echoed the roleblock or watch claims (sigilist, vigilante, sentinel), and an abstain option. All are unverified role claims, and the dead so far are an investigator and a concealed body, so nothing hard points at any candidate. Public vs private: You privately know the tracker's report of your visit is true and you blocked or visited that player; publicly it is only an unverified claim that you have denied or covered with a fabricated story.
  *Do:* When a claimed trailseer or other tracker publicly places your night visit on a target, and the room is abstaining or leaning against you, skip the revenge vote and join the room's consensus (abstain or the wagon's target). A solitary vote against your accuser changes no tally, repeats your accusation on the permanent record and marks you as the one player who wants the information role dead.
- key 2a3dca18-8e56-4791-94f6-6679ce9a40e5 (score 0.785) *When:* Late-game day vote where the chanteuse's side is losing. Either a fake-claim counter-claim contest has turned against you, or a credible watch or follow claim has doomed your wolf partner, and the room is converging. Information landscape: Information-rich from the town's view: confirmed roles for the dead, a lone killer possibly already eliminated, and claims corroborated by other claimants. You have nothing verifiable to offer. Stakes: The wolves are within a couple of eliminations of parity, so losing a wolf or exposing the pair is costly. This single vote will not flip the result, so the value is in keeping the wolves' public links minimal. Consensus: The village is largely aligned, with a lopsided majority forming on a claim-based case that looks consistent. There is no strong support for the minority view. My position: Holding out against, or being swept along by, a clear majority. Your vote is not the deciding one. Heat: High. You are either the target of a lost counter-claim with several players already naming you, or you recently pushed against a claimant who has since been corroborated. Forward exposure: Every vote is public and permanent. Matching your partner's vote, or opposing the room's clear pick, ties you to them and cannot be undone once the role is revealed. A vote that diverges costs little because your own vote cannot change a lopsided result. Target landscape: The realistic candidates are your counter-claimant, your exposed partner, and the Sentinel or other accuser. All are judged on claims and corroboration rather than hard proof, and the room has mostly settled on one. Public vs private: You privately know your partner's role and whether your own claim is false. The room is working from claims, counter-claims and past votes, and it sees you as either a liar or a defender of a likely wolf.
  *Do:* If the vote is already lost, because you are the losing side of a counter-claim or your partner is the target of a credible, room-wide case, cast the vote that least links you to your partner. Vote with the room when it is converging on your partner, and when you are the target pick a different name from your partner's. Votes are public and permanent, so a doomed vote that mirrors your partner's ties the two of you together.
- key 58ee1720-12e7-4d05-92ed-0c607d23f6f9 (score 0.777) *When:* Day 2 is the first vote of the game. Information is thin, with a night-1 death or a few unverified claims, and the village is split between piling on a target and abstaining. Information landscape: Starved: unverified claims, perhaps one revealed death, no confirmed roles and no vote history. Stakes: Early game with many players alive; no single vote decides the game, so staying unnoticed matters more than pushing an elimination. Consensus: The room is split or undecided between several targets and abstaining, with no evidence-based convergence. My position: With the majority or a plurality bloc, not driving. Heat: Low: you have not been accused and have only spoken mildly or generically. Forward exposure: A named vote is public and permanent and can later be read as wolf coordination if your partner is exposed. An abstain or a vote with the plurality commits you to little and carries low risk. Target landscape: Candidates are quiet players, unverified claimants, and abstaining. No roles are confirmed, so any named target is a behavior-based guess. Public vs private: You privately know both wolves and the pack's night plan, while the public has only unverified night-1 claims, one death at most, and behavioral reads, so nothing points at you.
  *Do:* On the first day-2 vote, when no roles are confirmed and you have low heat, cast your vote with the room's existing plurality, which is often abstain or a split. Keep your vote consistent with your partner's but do not drive the accusation. Voting with the room adds no new suspicion, and an unforced named vote is permanent record you cannot undo.

## Lookup 3024: luna game ae63e425, player_8 the trailseer, night_action, day 3
True roles (the reader's ground truth, not known to the seat): player_1 healer, player_2 serial_killer, player_3 sigilist, player_4 speculator, player_5 illusionist, player_6 vigilante, player_7 chanteuse, player_8 trailseer, player_9 investigator, player_10 sentinel

**Situation the seat wrote:** After the village eliminated a player revealed as the Illusionist, the trailseer faces a new target choice amid unresolved threats and competing role claims. A public save confirms that a player was attacked and protected, but not who provided the protection; the Investigator claim remains unverified, and a Not suspicious result does not rule out the Serial Killer. Earlier trail results establish no visits by the eliminated Illusionist or the roleblocked claimant on the respective nights, but do not identify the remaining threats. Information landscape: Mixed, with some direct but narrow evidence: your private trails found no visits by two players on separate nights, and a dead Sentinel’s publicly read record confirms a visit to the now-revealed Illusionist. Public reports establish a concealed night-one death from both wolves and the Serial Killer, a later Serial Killer attack stopped by protection, and the Illusionist’s revealed role; the concealed victim’s role remains unknown. Role claims and the identity of the healer who saved the attacked player remain unverified. Stakes: Seven players are alive, and the leading evil faction is five eliminations from parity at worst for the town; nevertheless, the game can end within one more elimination at worst for the town because the concealed body’s side is unknown. A single vote or kill can therefore flip which faction is winning, despite the stated parity distance. Target landscape: The remaining players are possible trail targets for distinguishing among the unresolved claims and suspicions: the player claiming Investigator has only an unverified Not suspicious result on a player whose possible Serial Killer status it does not clear; the player claiming Healer fits the public save, but that announcement does not identify who protected the target; the other living players have no publicly confirmed role status here. Your trail results directly establish that the now-eliminated Illusionist visited no one on Night 1 and that the claimed Investigator visited no one on Night 2.

**Lessons to judge:**
- key 6d3bf910-2e7f-4bde-8de2-4ea04eeb8cc9 (score 0.79) *When:* After a day elimination validated an investigative claim, the Trailseer chooses between auditing that claimant and chasing unknown suspects. The claimant may be a target for the wolves or the Chanteuse's block. Information landscape: Information-rich. There is a confirmed wolf flip from the Investigator's check, public claims, and a Sentinel watch that reported no visitors. Stakes: The remaining evil, a wolf and the lone killer, is still unknown. The Investigator's survival and credibility drive the village's votes, so verifying that they acted gives some value, but the follow adds no direct information on the remaining evil. Target landscape: A claimed Investigator whose check was validated by an elimination, versus a claimed Sentinel with a quiet watch result or unconfirmed quiet players. The choice rests on the value of verifying a key Town role.
  *Do:* When the Investigator's claim has just produced a confirmed result and the room trusts it, follow that Investigator to see whether they visited someone. If they visited no one, that matches a roleblock or a skipped check, but it is not proof of role. If they visited a player, you confirm they acted and learn who they checked.
- key 6c3b84f4-3b24-4596-aeb9-d540efaf2bf6 (score 0.782) *When:* The lone killer's kills come through a dead role's ability. The trailseer followed a quiet, unclaimed player and saw no visits, then treated the empty result as clearing them. Information landscape: Information-rich on role deaths, but the killer is unidentified, and an empty follow result is weak evidence because the killer's attack may be made through a body rather than its own visit. Stakes: With the wolves gone, the town can afford very few more mistakes. Treating one 'visited no one' as a clearance can steer the vote away from the killer and expose the trailseer's own standing, so the follow should narrow the field without being counted as proof of innocence. Target landscape: Both wolves dead; living players are a claimed sentinel, an unconfirmed healer, a neutral speculator, and a quiet unclaimed player. None is proven, and the choice rests on behavior and elimination of roles.
  *Do:* When the lone killer may be attacking through a dead player's body, as in a 'reanimated' morning report, follow the most suspicious survivor but record a 'visited no one' result as inconclusive. Keep the suspect on the list and choose the next night's follow from the remaining candidates, since a Necromancer's attack through a body would not appear as its own visit.
- key ad10b3a1-6ae2-4172-88d4-b7fae509ac57 (score 0.764) *When:* Late-game night with the wolves gone or nearly gone and a lone killer (Serial Killer) still hidden. The Trailseer must pick whom to follow among the remaining unexplained suspects to either implicate or clear them. Information landscape: Information-rich on identities, since most roles are revealed or deduced, but the last killer's identity is unresolved. A follow gives a hard visit trace (whom the target visited, or that they visited no one) that can settle the last unknown. Stakes: With few players left and a killer striking every night, one more night kill brings the killer or wolves close to winning. A correct follow can identify the killer for the next vote, so the pick of target is decisive for the Town win. Target landscape: Few unexplained survivors (2-3) remain after wolves and most roles are revealed or cleared. They are not role-confirmed, and the choice rests on behavior such as being quiet, deflecting, or being night-immune, plus elimination logic. One of them is the hidden lone killer.
  *Do:* When the wolves are gone or identified and one lone killer is still unrevealed, follow the unexplained survivor you most suspect of being the killer, choosing between the few remaining candidates. If they visited you on a night you were attacked, or visited another victim, that is direct contact evidence to bring forward. If they visited no one, that argues against them being the Serial Killer, so move your next follow to the other unexplained candidate.
